## SR-006 — Gate/tier harness enforces required steps

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall run the required steps of the gate that must next be passed (the strictness selector cached in docs/stage) and fail that gate when a required tool is missing, reporting SKIP(missing) rather than silently passing — and, on a claimed work branch (one whose history holds a docs/work/active/<branch>/ claim), SKIP its generated-artifact freshness steps with a stated trunk-lane reason, generated artifacts being trunk-only, while every non-freshness step still runs.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-004 (gates enforce their stage's checks), SN-008 (no false pass) and the dissolved edge expectation that a missing tool fails, never skips. A sanctioned SKIP class of this harness's own step plan is not a second crossing observable, it is the same one qualified. The skip is not a weakening: a work branch cannot commit generated artifacts at all (concurrency-restructure §5.2), so demanding their freshness there would force the very conflict the rule deletes; the skip is reported, never silent, and any signal ambiguity resolves toward the full trunk checks.


### LLR LLR-006
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Builds the required-step plan per gate/tier and reports SKIP(missing) with a nonzero exit when a required tool is absent; the plan listing still names every step. The work-branch lane skip is LLR-141, which decomposes from this same row.
  - after: Builds the required-step plan per gate and tier and reports SKIP(missing) with a nonzero exit when a required tool is absent; the plan listing still names every step. A gate run reports each step's result and exits 0, ending on its RESULT: PASS line, when no step fails, and nonzero, ending on its RESULT: FAIL line, when any step fails. Run over named steps, as the commit floor runs it, each named step runs leniently: its result is reported, a missing tool reads as a SKIP that does not fail the run, the run exits nonzero when any named step fails and 0 otherwise, and a name no step carries fails the run naming it. The work-branch lane skip is LLR-141, which decomposes from this same row.

## SR-007 — Declared stack profile, refused when it is broken

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall read the toolchain (format/lint/test commands, tiers, coverage, arch-map mode) from the declared stack profile so a stack swap edits only that declaration, failing loudly on a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold rather than proceeding on a bad one.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-003 — the kit is stack-agnostic; the harness is re-pointed by declaration, not by editing a kit script. Reading a declaration and REFUSING a broken one are one obligation, not two: a reader that proceeds on a malformed profile has not read it. A MISSING DECLARED BINARY is deliberately no part of it — SR-006 already states that verbatim as its own ("fail that gate when a required tool is missing"), which is why LLR-008 decomposes from both rows rather than one.

  - after: Reads the format, lint and test commands, the tier selectors, the coverage settings and the architecture-map mode from the declared stack profile and resolves each step's command from that profile. A project step the profile declares joins the plan with its command, from the stage it names, and an absent profile, section or key falls back to the harness's built-in reference.

## SR-017 — Always-on secrets floor

> **Requirement.** When a session attempts to commit or push content matching a secret pattern, the system shall refuse at the hook floor — scanning every commit's staged diff, message and outgoing range regardless of the privacy toggle, and naming the finding — unless the declared secrets_scan dial is explicitly false.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-009 — a credential that reaches a shared branch must be treated as disclosed and rotated, and expunging it from history is a rewrite every clone has to follow, so the cheap moment to catch it is before the commit and the expensive one is any moment after. Folding the scan into the privacy toggle was rejected: that makes the highest-severity check opt-in alongside a preference. The one opt-out is an explicit declaration, so turning it off is a visible act.


### LLR LLR-017

  - after: IF-032

### LLR LLR-493 — ADDED since the snapshot, Drafted — never accepted
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-493
- **SR-Refs**: SR-017;SR-018
- **Title**: The privacy scan names each leak by its location and class, and its exit code reports whether it found one
- **Detail**: Each leak the privacy scan finds, in the staged diff, a commit message, an outgoing commit range or the sweep over the tracked files, prints on its own line naming its location (a tracked file's path and line, or the commit) and its class label, and the run then prints one summary line counting the findings and naming the scan and the layers that ran; a clean run prints one clean line naming the scan and the layers. With both layers off it scans nothing, says so and exits 0. A scan it runs exits 1 when it printed a finding and 0 when it found none.
- **Rationale**: The hooks and the harness step read the scan's verdict from its exit code alone, and a person reads the lines to find the leak; naming the layers that ran keeps a run with a layer off from reading as a clean one.
- **TestRefs**: TC-586
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 6
- **Constrains**: IF-005;IF-043;IF-148

## SR-018 — Privacy gate two-axis

> **Requirement.** When the declared privacy_check dial is on, the system shall additionally refuse, at the same hook floor, a commit or push carrying a PII/identity class — exempting configured author identities.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-009 — identity and PII classes are a policy choice a repo makes about its own contributors, unlike a leaked credential, which is a defect in every repo. Making them always-on was rejected because the exemption list they need is project-specific: a repo that has not declared its author identities would have the layer firing on its own commit metadata, and the fix would be to silence the scanner rather than to configure it.

## SR-019 — Pre-commit hook floor

> **Requirement.** The pre-commit hook shall run the integrity + secrets floor on every commit, agent-neutrally — a LOCAL floor, bypassable by the session (git commit --no-verify), so it discharges "no unchecked write enters governed state" only as a PAIR with the hosted re-run of the same checks (SR-151/SR-152), never alone.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-005 and SN-009 — a floor only CI enforces is one the local tree crosses freely, and a secret caught after the commit is already in history. Trusting the agent to run the checks was rejected: a floor exists for the times the agent forgets, so it is git-enforced and agent-neutral. Parents the interpreter probe: a hook that crashes cryptically because no python3 resolves, or because a Windows Store alias exits nonzero, has not run the floor — so probing by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly is machinery serving THIS obligation, and it decomposes to LLR-021 rather than standing as a requirement of its own.


### LLR LLR-019
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Runs trace --strict-integrity and the check_privacy secrets scan on staged content and blocks a failing commit. Every built-in check step is classified exactly once as run in the commit floor, run by the hook by name, or not run at commit with a stated reason. When no kit scripts directory is findable, the commit-facing hooks refuse the commit with exit 1, name the available repair steps, and neither run nor skip the floor.
  - after: Runs the registry integrity check and the secrets scan on staged content and refuses, with a nonzero exit, a commit failing either; a commit passing both clears those two checks, and whether it is admitted rests on the floor's other steps. Every built-in check step is classified exactly once as run in the commit floor, run by the hook by name, or not run at commit with a stated reason. When no kit scripts directory is findable, the commit-facing hooks refuse the commit with exit 1, name the available repair steps, and neither run nor skip the floor.

## SR-024 — Permutation case generation

> **Requirement.** The delivered permutation-case generator shall expand a Permutations spec into concrete dimensional cases.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-002 — dimensional coverage is generated from the SR's declared inputs, not hand-listed. Systematic expansion reduces the risk that a dimensional combination is omitted, which is the charter's coverage half. Generation does NOT answer the charter's second half: showing that an enforcer fails when it should is demonstrated by negative or planted-failure tests, and a hand-listed negative case can demonstrate it. Those negative tests remain a separate obligation, not this row's.

## SR-026 — Coordinator resumes headless

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordinator shall resume headless with stdin closed, never blocking on a prompt: a worker resumes from its explicit claimed assignment plus the committed trailer evidence on its branch, and the integrator derives claim and queue state from trunk history alone — the generated status surface never a session input.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run. The acceptance is deliberately narrower than it once was: it had carried two obligations the shall never stated - transient-limit backoff and stall abort - and an acceptance cell cannot mint a requirement. Both were MINTED rather than deleted, because each describes live behaviour worth keeping: SR-171 and SR-172. An unattended run blocked on a prompt is indistinguishable from a hung one and holds its lane until an operator notices. Keeping a separate serial resume path beside the dispatcher was rejected: two authorities over one decision drift, and the divergence surfaces as a run that resumes differently depending on how it was started.


### LLR LLR-026
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Runs the agent CLI headless: build_argv substitutes {model} into argv and, when a CmdTemplate carries no {prompt} placeholder (the default for both provider CLIs), routes the prompt to the child's STDIN (run_session writes it from a daemon thread then closes), while the no-wedge invariant holds — a fixed prompt plus EOF, never an interactive read. A codex session additionally captures that CLI's own --output-last-message file as the result (_codex_lastmsg_setup/_codex_lastmsg_read). stdin is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status.md-resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them. An interruption ends the tree only once the coordinator supervises the session; one that lands while the session is still being launched is outside the kill. A wall or idle deadline, or an interruption while the coordinator supervises the session, ends every process in the session process group on POSIX or session job on Windows: the direct child is reaped, then the group or job is polled until empty. On Windows the kill takes a handle on each member, then terminates the job, and returns only when the job count is zero and every held member is signalled; an unopenable live member raises after the terminate. Past 10 s the kill raises naming the survivor. A failure to end the tree, or to show that it ended, raises the named tree-end error; an ordinary launch error still returns the session error. A child that cannot be contained ends as a session error before it runs.
  - after: Runs the agent command-line tool headless: the launch substitutes the routed model into the command's tokens and, when the command template carries no prompt slot (the default for both provider tools), delivers the prompt on the child's standard input, written from a background thread and then closed, while the no-wedge invariant holds: a fixed prompt plus end-of-file, never an interactive read. A Codex session also captures that tool's own last-message file as the result. Standard input is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status-file resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them. An interruption ends the tree only once the coordinator supervises the session; one that lands while the session is still being launched is outside the kill. A wall or idle deadline, or an interruption while the coordinator supervises the session, ends every process in the session's process group on POSIX or its session job on Windows: the direct child is reaped, then the group or job is polled until empty. On Windows the kill takes a handle on each member, then terminates the job, and returns only when the job count is zero and every held member is signalled; an unopenable live member raises after the terminate. Past 10 s the kill raises naming the survivor. A failure to end the tree, or to show that it ended, raises the named tree-end error; an ordinary launch error still returns the session error. A child that cannot be contained ends as a session error before it runs.
- **Rationale**
  - before: A large prompt passed as argv dies at the OS command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows .CMD shim, 32767 via CreateProcess) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so stdin is the only delivery that survives — both limits verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that CLI echoes its banner and the whole prompt into stdout, so stdout is not the result. A surviving descendant can keep editing a worktree after its parent session ends; process-group and job containment make the tree an atomic session lifetime, while an uncontained Windows child is rejected before it can create that survivor. Holding each member before termination covers the measured interval where the job count reaches zero before a terminated process is signalled.
  - after: A large prompt passed on the command line dies at the operating system's command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows command shim, 32767 through direct process creation) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so standard input is the only delivery that survives; both limits were verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that tool echoes its banner and the whole prompt into standard output, so standard output is not the result. A surviving descendant can keep editing a worktree after its parent session ends; process-group and job containment make the tree an atomic session lifetime, while an uncontained Windows child is rejected before it can create that survivor. Holding each member before termination covers the measured interval where the job count reaches zero before a terminated process is signalled.

## SR-028 — Coordinator typed outcomes

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordinator shall end each session in a typed outcome code, guard a zero-commit HEAD, and report an all-ERROR stall as an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectations that a repository with no commits yet (HEAD absent) does not crash the loop and that an all-ERROR run is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall — a coordinator ending in an untyped code forces its caller to parse prose; a session that produced no commit still has a HEAD to report, so without a guard an empty run is indistinguishable from a productive one in the record it leaves; and an all-ERROR run reported as a work stall sends an operator looking for a blocked work item that does not exist. Treating an agent error as a stall was rejected for that reason: an unreachable agent and a blocked item need different responses, so they need different outcomes.


### LLR LLR-028
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Sites SR-028's obligation in the coordinator loop as three mechanisms: the outcome ladder that resolves every session end state to one typed code, the zero-commit HEAD guard, and the stall fork whose all-ERROR verdict is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.
  - after: Sites SR-028's obligation in the coordinator loop as three mechanisms: the outcome ladder that resolves every session end state to one typed code, the zero-commit HEAD guard, and the stall fork whose all-ERROR verdict is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall. The coordinator stops with the exit code assigned to the end state it reached, so its caller reads why it stopped from the code alone.

## SR-031 — Declared-policy readers agree

> **Requirement.** Every delivered enforcer of a declared policy dial shall read the same value for that dial as every other enforcer — one parse, one answer, across the hooks, the loop and the checkers alike.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-028's one readable policy home. A policy answer calculated more than once can diverge across enforcers, leaving the operator unable to know which value governs; one parse gives every enforcer the same answer. SR-137 owns whether a declaration is ambiguous or invalid, while this row owns agreement among the enforcers that consume a valid declaration.


### LLR LLR-031
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: All policy enforcers, including the agent layer, obtain a declared policy answer from one parsed representation. A privacy gate declaration the parse holds outside `[policies]` reads as gate on, whatever its value or any direct or legacy gate declaration; a privacy reviewer declaration it holds there reads as reviewer required, whatever its value or any direct or legacy reviewer declaration. A name inside a string declares nothing. An unreadable or unparseable declaration is a distinct outcome, never a default. Only the exact opt-down value directly under the policies table opts the unwired reviewer down; a copy elsewhere or an unreadable declaration requires the reviewer, and the legacy record answers only when that key is absent. The hook answer is one of: the gate off; on with the unwired reviewer required; on with it warned; a mixed declaration; or an unreadable declaration, as IF-298 carries. The hooks no longer read a declared line themselves and instead reach it through that answer.
  - after: All policy enforcers, including the agent layer, obtain a declared policy answer from one parsed representation. A privacy gate declaration the parse holds outside the policies table reads as gate on, whatever its value or any direct or legacy gate declaration; a privacy reviewer declaration it holds there reads as reviewer required, whatever its value or any direct or legacy reviewer declaration. Only the exact opt-down value directly under the policies table opts the unwired reviewer down; a copy elsewhere or an unreadable declaration requires the reviewer, and the legacy record answers only when that key is absent. The hook answer is one of: the gate off; on with the unwired reviewer required; on with it warned; a mixed declaration; or an unreadable declaration, as IF-298 carries. The hooks no longer read a declared line themselves and instead reach it through that answer.

## SR-032 — Onboarding and dev-setup scaffold

> **Requirement.** The onboarding and dev-setup templates shall scaffold and run to a passing developer setup.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-001 — a new contributor who cannot reach a working setup from the repo alone is blocked on whoever set it up last, and onboarding prose that is never executed drifts from the commands that work. A README checklist was rejected: nothing fails when it goes stale.


### LLR LLR-320
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The --for-run/-ForRun operation reports workstation readiness first, querying a workstation tool only once it is found present and reporting an absent one as missing, so an absent tool never ends the report before its runtime result; it then offers each missing setup item only at an interactive terminal, and without one it offers nothing and does not consume menu input. Its runtime is the interpreter the launcher hands it, never one it searches for: it exits 0 when that interpreter satisfies the runtime floor and otherwise, including when none is handed, exits 1 with the step to take. The standalone --check/-Check operation reports only, performs no offer or write, and exits 0.
  - after: Dev-setup's readiness operation for a bare run reports workstation readiness first, querying a workstation tool only once it is found present and reporting an absent one as missing, so an absent tool never ends the report before its runtime result; it then offers each missing setup item only at an interactive terminal, and without one it offers nothing and does not consume menu input. Its runtime is the interpreter the launcher hands it, never one it searches for: it exits 0 when that interpreter satisfies the runtime floor and otherwise, including when none is handed, exits 1 with the step to take. The standalone readiness check reports only, performs no offer or write, and exits 0.

## SR-033 — Release checklist generation

> **Requirement.** The delivered release-checklist generator shall emit the release-gate checklist, including the warn-tier performance budgets that never fail a gate and differentiable assumption confirmations.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-004 — the release gate has a generated checklist surfacing the budgets a human must tick off, because a warn-tier budget that never fails a gate is read by nobody unless something puts it in front of a reader. No wider than what the charter actually asks: the charter asks what happens when a budget is exceeded, and for a warn-tier budget the mechanical answer is "nothing" — so this row is this project's ANSWER to the charter's question, not an obligation the charter imposes. The charter prescribes neither a checklist nor a human tick-off, and the derivation stated here must not be read as though it did. The assumptions section realizes SN-043 at the same gate: a premise can only be shown false by someone looking for its falsifier, so the checklist puts each active accepted assumption, and any with no falsifier, in front of the person signing, marked apart from the requirement checks.

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordinator shall route each in-process session phase (PLAN/BUILD/REVIEW-A/REVIEW-B/DESIGN-CHECK/CRITIQUE) through its declared per-phase command template, falling back to the single declared command — surfacing the declared reviewer dial at run start without enforcing it.

Dispatch stays keyed off the in-process session phase because the alternative, a per-phase code branch, makes adding a phase a code change and so discourages adding one. THIS ROW CARRIED A RESUME-SURFACE SIZE TRIPWIRE AND NO LONGER DOES, and the two lenses that reached it are answered here rather than deleted with it. Both were premised on a session INHERITING a resume surface that grows across runs: a view must stay usable as its content volume grows (hat.UX-ENGINEER), and at 3am the reader of that surface is another automated step, which needs the growth legible from the artifact rather than from a human noticing (hat.UNATTENDED-OPS). Neither premise survives the one-engine coordinator — no session inherits a resume surface any more, and the status page is a generated integrator artifact whose size the generator owns, so there is no growing view to keep usable and no 3am artifact whose growth an automated reader must judge. The lenses are not overruled; their subject is gone. If a per-session resume surface is ever reintroduced, both apply again o… [187 more chars — read the registry row]

## SR-043 — Subagent spawn gate

> **Requirement.** While an unattended run is in progress, the delivered subagent-spawn gate shall refuse or defer subagent spawns per the declared subagent-gate dial in the single policy declaration home (off|ask|deny; absent=off; the legacy one-word file is still read through the migration window), honor a launcher-set SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override the model cannot set, and fail open on any error so a broken gate never wedges the tools.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run stays safe — bounded, supervised fan-out with the override held by the operator, not the model) and SN-012 (opt-in: absent/off is a vacuous allow, so a non-adopter pays nothing). Supervision, not security — a model that can edit files can remove the hook (adapted from stop-subagent-fanout, MIT; spec docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4). C-SEC-2 asks that the irreversible actions an unattended run can take be enumerated, each naming the dial or operator authority that permits it — which is this row exactly, for the one action that starts further actors. A direct hit, and the reason this row is not accretion: its parentage was real and lived only in SN-006's `why` cell until the safety half moved into that need's normative text at this same pass. WHY THE FAIL-OPEN ARM IS DELIBERATE: SN-006 asks that the unattended layer keep moving and surface to the operator only where it cannot proceed, so a gate that wedges every tool on its own bug is the failure this clause was written against; what an error never relaxes is the operator-held override itself, which the model cannot set.


### LLR LLR-040
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Pure decide(tool_name, policy, override) -> allow|ask|deny|defer over SPAWN_TOOLS={Task,Agent}; main reads the PreToolUse JSON payload, reads docs/process.toml [checks] subagent_gate (falling back to the legacy one-word docs/subagent-gate through the migration window), applies the SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override, emits permissionDecision (deny -> exit 2), and appends each decision to out/subagent-gate.log; any exception fails OPEN (allow) and logs, so a broken gate never wedges the agent's tools.
  - after: A pure decision maps a tool call's name, the declared gate policy and an allow override to allow, ask, deny or defer, deferring every call that names neither of the agent tool's two subagent-spawn tools. Run as the agent tool's pre-tool-use hook, the gate reads the call's payload from standard input, reads the gate dial from the process configuration's checks table (falling back to the legacy one-word declaration through the migration window) and applies the allow override set in its environment. The dial off, or no dial declared in either home, allows a spawn; ask asks for acceptance; deny refuses it; any other value asks; and a process configuration that is present but does not parse asks, or refuses where the legacy declaration denies. It prints the decision as the agent tool's permission-decision payload, a deny exiting 2 and every other decision 0, and prints nothing for a deferred call; each decision but a defer is appended to the gate's decision log as one tab-separated line naming the tool and the decision. Any error fails open, allowing the call and logging why, so a broken gate never wedges the agent's tools.

## SR-046 — Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository root

> **Requirement.** The launchers at the repository root shall expose every declared runnable capability consistently to an interactive operator, to a direct caller naming one capability, and to automated discovery - from a single declaration, on Windows and POSIX alike.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-001 - the evaluator's rungs make running the product never depend on recalling a command, and the capability set is declared once in docs/stack.ini rather than hard-wired and duplicated across the platform launchers. ONE OBLIGATION, not a menu specification: the row states the one capability-level decision - every declared capability is reachable, the same way, from one declaration, by three kinds of caller - because numbering, listing format, declaration grammar, fallback text and exit handling fail independently, which makes an enumeration of them a decomposition wearing a requirement's clothes. Those are LLR-047's and the acceptance's.


### LLR LLR-047
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: load_capabilities parses the docs/stack.ini [run] section (configparser, interpolation=None, case-preserving optionxform; declaration order kept; a name.desc row attaches to its command and an orphan or empty-value command is dropped) into an ordered (name, command, desc) list; --list prints name<TAB>desc per row; direct(name) launches by exact-match name (exit 2 on an unknown name) and launch() runs the shell line via subprocess shell=True, passing the child exit code straight through. launch prepends to PATH a directory whose bare python resolves to the menu interpreter: on Windows that interpreter's own directory, and on POSIX a per-launch temporary directory of executable shims that exec its exact path. On Windows the command processor searches the line's working directory before PATH, so a python.exe there, whether at the repository root or in a directory the line enters, runs instead and is outside this guarantee; when that directory has no interpreter, the prepended directory governs PATH lookup. On POSIX that directory also makes bare python3 exact; a line intended to run on Windows declares python. The POSIX construction is a shim rather than a link so a virtual environment retains its configuration. interactive_menu prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from stdin, and returns EOF-safe without hanging; an absent or empty [run] prints guidance and exits 1. Every run launcher form resolves one interpreter by running, in order, its repository virtual-environment candidates and then the candidates from its dev-setup's ordinary runtime search, taking the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it runs the menu on exactly that path. The no-argument launchers change to the repository root and invoke dev-setup's --for-run/-ForRun readiness operation once before the menu, handing it that interpreter when one resolves; a nonzero result prints guidance and leaves the menu unopened. Direct and --list forms bypass that operation and, on Windows, the closing pause, and exit 1 with guidance when no interpreter resolves; run.command delegates to run.sh so it does not duplicate the operation, and non-interactive menu input remains available after it.
  - after: The run menu reads the run section of the stack profile into a list of name, command and description in declaration order, with no interpolation, so a percent sign in a value is literal, and keeping each name's case; a name's description row attaches to its command, and a description row naming no command, or a command with an empty value, is dropped. Its list form prints one line per capability: the name, a tab and the description. A capability named on the command line is launched by exact name, an unknown name exiting 2, and the launch runs the declared line through the shell and passes the child's exit code straight through. The launch puts first on the search path a directory whose bare python resolves to the menu's own interpreter: on Windows that interpreter's own directory, and on POSIX a per-launch temporary directory of executable shims that run its exact path. On Windows the command processor searches the line's working directory before the search path, so a python executable there, at the repository root or in a directory the line enters, runs instead and is outside this guarantee; with no interpreter there, the prepended directory governs the search. On POSIX that directory also makes bare python3 exact, and a line meant to run on Windows declares python; the POSIX construction is a shim rather than a link so a virtual environment keeps its configuration. With no argument it prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from standard input, and returns without hanging when the input is closed; an absent or empty run section prints guidance and exits 1. Every run launcher resolves one interpreter by running, in order, its repository virtual-environment candidates and then the candidates of its development setup's ordinary runtime search, taking the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path, and runs the menu on exactly that path. The no-argument launchers change to the repository root and invoke the development setup's readiness operation for a bare run once before the menu, handing it that interpreter when one resolves; a nonzero result prints guidance and leaves the menu unopened. The direct and list forms bypass that operation and, on Windows, the closing pause, and exit 1 with guidance when no interpreter resolves; the macOS launcher delegates to the POSIX one so it does not duplicate the operation, and non-interactive menu input remains available after it.
- **Rationale**
  - before: The command being run is the user's own declared line, so shell=True is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. EOF-safety is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed stdin turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.
  - after: The command being run is the user's own declared line, so running it through the shell is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. Safety at end-of-file is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed standard input turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.

  - after: IF-048;IF-157;IF-158;IF-049

## SR-049 — Derived stage from artifact states

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall derive the stage the project is currently at from the spine artifact states alone, caching it as a derived value — never accepting a hand-set one — with a post-acceptance amendment dropping the derived stage exactly as a newly introduced draft does, and the pending-amendment count carried beside the draft count so the pending state never hides.

> **Rationale.** SSOT applied to the gate itself: a hand-set marker can be bumped without the work; deriving it makes gate advancement mechanical (SN-004) and un-gameable (SN-008). The hybrid cache keeps the value readable on checkout; --check guards rot. SN-029 is a parent because this row's shall is the only place in the SR layer stating that an amended requirement drops the derived stage exactly as a newly introduced one does — SR-139 states which tiers are held, SR-140 what an acceptance record contains, and SR-148 loop selection, none of them the drop clause.


### LLR LLR-118
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_

## SR-070 — Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkable

> **Requirement.** The delivered generator set shall derive each artifact it produces from the tracked registries alone — self-contained and usable without network access, byte-stable across regeneration from unchanged sources, and carrying a freshness contract that fails when the committed copy has drifted from those sources — omitting a view whose source registries the adopting repository does not carry rather than emitting an empty one.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-010 and the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check, by isolating one independently reviewable obligation formerly carried by SR-038; with SN-012, since a repository that carries no source registry pays nothing for the view that registry would have fed. ONE DECISION PER ROW: the INTEGRITY of a generated artifact and what the state view must SHOW are two unrelated obligations, and this row holds the first alone. They fail independently: a view can be perfectly offline, deterministic and drift-checkable while showing nothing about the work in flight or how the parts connect. The content half sheds along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws ("progress AND how its parts connect") to SR-168 and SR-169; this row keeps the artifact-integrity contract alone. The subject is "each artifact the generator set produces" rather than "each view" deliberately: the knowledge-export bundle is not a view, and a fourth row minted to hold it would be the filler tier the ruling forbids. The child/parent phase spread is untouched and is intended — this split is by observable class, not by phase, a partition that was considered and rejected.


### LLR LLR-039
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: emit() builds {relpath: content} for every real row (sorted, deterministic, no timestamps): frontmatter via JSON-quoted YAML scalars, bodies carrying the row prose plus up/down markdown links from locally built reverse maps, per-tier and root indexes, the UPSTREAM.md spec pin; on_disk() snapshots docs/okf for the byte-compare; write mode writes-if-changed and prunes; small loaders duplicated per the F5 rule (no new sibling import). Layer B2 adds a process-guides tier: _doc_title_and_summary derives each guide title+summary from the doc first heading/paragraph (inline links stripped so nothing dangles), emitted only after the vacuity gate so a fresh scaffold stays vacuous. Graph links resolve within the bundle; each Process Guide carries a resource pointer to its source doc, which is left unmodified. --check trips on a registry or doc edit, a missing bundle file and a hand-added extra, recovering after regeneration; docs/process.toml [checks] okf_export = false silences the step, with the legacy docs/okf-export sentinel still read through the SN-028 migration window; off and placeholder-only registries pass silently, and process guides emit only alongside a real spine.
  - after: The bundle generator builds one file per real registry row, sorted and deterministic with no timestamp: each file's front matter carries the row's cells as JSON-quoted strings, which YAML reads as scalars, and its body the row's prose with links up and down the spine, beside per-tier and root indexes and a pin of the bundle format's specification. Its write mode writes a file only when its content changed and prunes a file the bundle no longer holds. A process-guides tier gives each present guide document a title and summary taken from its first heading and paragraph, inline links stripped so nothing dangles, and points at its source document, which is left unmodified; guides are emitted only alongside a real spine, so a fresh scaffold stays vacuous. Every link in the bundle resolves within it. The freshness check fails after a registry edit, after an edit that changes a guide's derived title or summary, on a missing bundle file and on a hand-added extra file, and passes again after regeneration. The process settings' bundle opt-out silences the step; where the process settings declare no bundle choice, a legacy one-word opt-out file reading off silences it too, and a declared choice overrides that file. A registry holding only example rows passes silently.

The cost is real and is accepted rather than hidden: a requirement list says what a component is obliged to do and nothing about how its parts fit, so the artifact that stays true is bought at the price of the artifact that showed mechanism. ## SR-112 — Checked per-agent skill fan-out

> **Requirement.** The per-agent skill copies shall be a checked, generated fan-out of the one neutral skill source.

> **Rationale.** Realizes the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check — one skill maintained in three agent-specific copies rots into three different skills, and the copy an agent actually loads is the one nobody edited. Maintaining a single shared directory was rejected because each agent harness looks in its own path; the fan-out is forced, so the copies are generated and drift is a finding. Generated marking is what stops a maintainer editing a disposable copy.

  - after: Reads each skill's applicability front matter from its entry document and regenerates the skills index from it. The regenerated index reflects the current front matter, and a drifted index is detectable.

The agent layer's write-once copy for the chosen agents copies those pairs, and the delivered-package inventory lists the same pairs as the skill's conditional deliveries, so the scaffold copy and the inventory read one statement of what a skill ships.

  - before: The fan-out drift check compares the whole file set of a skill against its source, so a first copy of the SKILL.md alone is drifted from the moment it is written: the skill arrives without the file it cites, and the check fails until a refresh repairs it. Stating the unit once, and having the copy and the inventory both read it, keeps the scaffold, the refresh and the drift check agreeing on one file set; a directory walk written into each caller was the alternative, and it would let the two lists disagree.
  - after: The fan-out drift check compares the whole file set of a skill against its source, so a first copy of the entry document alone is drifted from the moment it is written: the skill arrives without the file it cites, and the check fails until a refresh repairs it. Stating the unit once, and having the copy and the inventory both read it, keeps the scaffold, the refresh and the drift check agreeing on one file set; a directory walk written into each caller was the alternative, and it would let the two lists disagree.

## SR-139 — Acceptance as an ordinal over a derived spine stage

> **Requirement.** The kit shall express operator acceptance authority as a cumulative level naming the highest spine tier an operator still accepts, compared against a separately derived SPINE STAGE via a declared, auditable rung ordering — every unreadable or out-of-range input resolving toward MORE operator involvement.

> **Rationale.** The retired three-value enum could not express "TCs are operator-held but LLRs are not", and four tables each re-interpreted the same word with its own fail-safe direction. The failure that matters is a machine accepting something an operator meant to hold, so every degrade has to point the same way — including the ones that look like kindness: clamping an out-of-range level toward 0 reads as "nothing is operator-held" and disarms every hold in the repo. The crossing attribution: B-02 is unarguable - the acceptance level IS the authority input, and every fail-safe clause here describes how that input is read when it is broken. B-05 earns its place on a test, not on the fact that everything here is delivered content: the acceptance clause "Level N holds exactly the tiers the SHIPPED TEMPLATE documents" is a claim about the package's own contents agreeing with its behaviour, which is falsifiable against the package alone with no authority act performed - the same package-wide property class SR-031 occupies. The objection this had to survive is the measured one: B-05 already carries about four fifths of the layer's references, so adding a row to it needs an observable that is genuinely AT the package, not a default. This row has one. That the imbalance itself may be wrong is a separate question about the frame rather than about this row, and it is recorded where such questions belong.


### LLR LLR-259
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_

The page's title claims no operator act, and its signing instruction covers only the sections outside the block. At a level that releases no spine tier, no collapsed block renders and every owing chain stays in the operator's section.
  - after: The operator's acceptance brief asks the shared acceptance-dial predicate once per spine tier (requirements, design rows and test cases), each tier's registry taken from the spine's registry list, and takes the tiers the declared acceptance level releases; the brief holds no rung table of its own. A chain of the acceptance model is released when at least one of its rows owes an act (a Drafted row, or a row changed, added or removed against the accepted snapshot) and every such row is on a released tier; one owing row on a held tier, or no owing row at all, keeps the chain held. The brief renders every owing chain: the held ones in the operator's section as before, or, when every owing chain is released, a line in the operator's section stating that no chain on a held rung owes an act, so the brief never reads as nothing owed above the collapsed block; then, when any chain is released, a heading and one details block, closed by default and labelled "Waiting for automated adjudication" with the chains' ids in its summary, holding each released chain rendered in full by the same per-chain renderer. The page's title claims no operator act, and its signing instruction covers only the sections outside the block. At a level that releases no spine tier, no collapsed block renders and every owing chain stays in the operator's section.

## SR-144 — Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first, and refusing a close whose claim is other than one work item.

> **Rationale.** Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence once merged rejected code onto trunk: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable. A claim a dead lane never releases is C-UNA-3's deadlocked-until-morning case, and the immutable per-close record is C-UNA-5's answer to a failure that pages nobody — a close no one can read is externally indistinguishable from a run that finished.


### LLR LLR-167
- **Detail**
  - before: The route reads `Brief` and `Adjudicates` through the same normalization the merge uses, so a spelling or scope accepted by one cannot change the other’s bound. The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint that has a brief to declare; the report-less cancellation and clean-close spot-check arms declare none); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `evidence_gap_values` RE-RUNS `census.evidence_gap_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `census.parse_evidence_gap` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal is a HOLD, never a fallback: `session_body` returns the reason and `agent_loop.route_session` prints it on the stop banner and returns `EXIT_NEEDS_OPERATOR` with no session run, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. EVERY shipped brief is routed through an assembler, including done-when and combined: `conflict` was RETIRED rather than filled (it had a template and a verdict grammar and never a mint, an assembler or a reader for the `needs=` its own grammar demanded), and `consolidate_values` composes the consolidation brief from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived LIVE - every row of that cluster or none, and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved refuses rather than briefing a session about a contradiction that no longer exists. `amendment_values` renders the accepted-cell before/after of the drifted rows `trace.reaccept_model` finds, BOUNDED by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes exactly as the first-acceptance mint does: a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree, a scope none of whose rows still differs from its accepted copy refuses naming them, and a row hanging under several SRs is rendered once, naming each chain; a scoped row that the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal for the sitting to rule, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a drifted row's tier, and its identity the digest of its recorded accepted text; a scope token naming a row that neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses the composition, naming the token; and a scoped row present live with no accepted-cell drift drops out as before; its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's snapshot copy (`baseline_snapshot.stamp` given the registry), never the newest write anywhere in the snapshot directory. For need, assumption and surrogate scopes, _unchained_amended_rows renders their drifted accepted cells through tier_owing, and the amendment aftermath takes its held-rung arm from adjudication_action. QUESTION HOME. `tier_questions` reads `prompts/spine-questions.md` at render time for the `{questions}` slot that `first_acceptance_values` and `amendment_values` return. Each `## ` section's next line declares its served tiers as `<!-- tiers: ... -->`, with `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, every-tier sections and sections serving the rows judged: `_judged_tiers` derives first-acceptance tiers from its `--accepts` registry walk, and amendment tiers from the rows shown. `combined_values` composes each section through `compose`, so its first-acceptance and amendment sections carry their own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section without a tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief naming that home; there is no fallback. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives neither an acceptance-registry token nor its per-token row map, and amendment aftermath identifies each verdict mark for a later turn. A CLARITY verdict is later re-anchored, naming its verdict on a held rung; a released-rung MEANING verdict it blesses is marked `MEANING+BLESS`; a held MEANING verdict is recommended to the operator.
  - after: The session route reads an adjudication row's `Brief` and `Adjudicates` cells through the same reading the merge slot uses, so a spelling or scope one accepts cannot move the other's bound. The row's declared `Brief` cell selects the brief's template: the trunk mint writes it at every adjudication it mints with a brief to declare, its report-less cancellation and clean-close spot-check rows declare none, and a row declaring an unknown brief, the retired conflict brief included, refuses. The brief is filled under the prompt loader's strict check, which refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared one, and both of the loop's routing arms reach it by one path, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Every shipped brief, the Done-when and combined briefs included, is composed whole by its own evidence derivation or refused with a reason, never in part. A refusal is a hold, never a fallback: the loop prints the reason on its stop banner and exits asking for an operator, running no session, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. A session handed a judging brief completes only when it commits, under the row's result trailer, the typed verdict line the brief demands at the path the brief names; no verdict, a missing machine line, a label outside the brief's closed set or a missing counter leaves it incomplete. The disposition brief follows the row's `SpecRef` to the closed spec, its immutable per-close report and that report's typed commit range, and lists the range's commits and changed paths clipped at a declared 80 lines; it refuses a clean close, which writes no report, and a report with no typed commit range. The evidence-gap brief re-runs the evidence-gap census rather than remembering it, joins each finding to its test case's method, expected and evidence cells and to the requirement or design text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean, a test case missing a listed cell and a target with no normative text. The consolidation brief is composed from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived live, every row of it or none; it refuses a row with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. The amendment brief is bounded by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes, as the first-acceptance mint does; a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree. For each scoped row that claims acceptance and differs from its recorded copy in an accepted cell or an adjudicated link (LLR-484), it shows each moved accepted cell and each moved adjudicated link by its cell name with its before and after, a link marked as one, so a row whose only moved cells are adjudicated links is shown; a row under several requirements is shown once, naming each chain. It asks of each link whether the row's text states what the link now points at. A moved trace cell no adjudicator judges is not shown. A scoped row the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a shown row's tier. Need, assumption and surrogate rows in scope are shown with their moved accepted cells by the one owing rule. A scope token naming a row neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses, naming the token; a scope none of whose rows has anything to judge refuses, naming the scope; and each other scoped row with nothing to judge is named as not shown. Its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's recorded copy, not the newest write anywhere in the record; it states one judged-text identity line per row shown (LLR-359); and its aftermath takes each verdict's held-rung arm from the one home of what a verdict owes on a held rung. The questions slot of the first-acceptance and amendment briefs is read at render time from the shipped questions home, one section per heading, each declaring on its next line the tiers it serves, `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, the sections serving every tier and those serving a judged tier: a first acceptance's tiers are those of the registries its rows to accept sit in, an amendment's those of the rows shown, and a combined sitting composes each section as its own brief, so each carries its own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section with no tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief, naming the home; there is no fallback, and no prompt template or copy of the spine-authoring skill restates a question of it. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives no registry argument for the act nor the rows each would cover, and the amendment aftermath names, for a later act turn, the mark each verdict owes: a CLARITY row is re-anchored later, naming its verdict on a held rung; a MEANING row whose new text the adjudicator would bless on a released rung is marked MEANING+BLESS; a held MEANING row is recommended to the operator.
- **Rationale**
  - before: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-OPERATOR` fold (LLR-161).
  - after: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived, and the one claim shown, the lane's report, is the template's own labelled subject. And a half-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than the reference cell's shape, because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap finding both cite the test-case registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a title substring, the fold LLR-161 retired. The amendment brief shows a moved adjudicated link beside the accepted cells because an act naming the row records it whole from live (LLR-245): a link the brief did not show would enter the record under a verdict that never saw it, and a scoped row left out unnamed lets a verdict and its scope disagree about what was judged.

## SR-146 — Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail

> **Requirement.** Every prompt the delivered loop launches or an attended launcher renders as a review or critique brief shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each loop session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.

> **Rationale.** Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.) C-SEC-5 requires that content composed for dispatch to an external model runner carry a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — a prompt assembled inside source is an unreviewed egress path no write-side gate covers, and shipping it as a reviewable file with a digest is what makes that rule readable.


### LLR LLR-162
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: `load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `KIT_FRAGMENTS` declares each composed fragment and its slot; `fragment_rows` reads each through the shared `_read_body`, digests its normalized text, and raises `PromptError` when it is absent, unreadable or empty rather than omitting it or digesting it as empty. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
  - after: The prompt loader reads a shipped template, or an operator override in its place, and refuses with a named prompt error; the strict fill refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; the prompt digest normalizes line endings before hashing; and the launch preflight turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. The kit declares each composed fragment and the slot it fills; the catalogue's fragment rows read each through the loader's shared body reading, digest its normalized text, and refuse with the prompt error when it is absent, unreadable or empty rather than omitting it or digesting it as empty. The catalogue's prompt rows give, sorted by key, each shipped prompt's key, file, slots and digest, and its fragment rows give each fragment's file, the slot it fills and its digest. The catalogue generator renders the catalogue with a freshness check, and each session's record carries the fingerprint of the rendered result beside the template's own name.
