Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `evidence_gap_values` RE-RUNS `census.evidence_gap_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `census.parse_evidence_gap` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal is a HOLD, never a fallback: `session_body` returns the reason and `agent_loop.route_session` prints it on the stop banner and returns `EXIT_NEEDS_OPERATOR` with no session run, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. EVERY shipped brief is routed through an assembler, including done-when and combined: `conflict` was RETIRED rather than filled (it had a template and a verdict grammar and never a mint, an assembler or a reader for the `needs=` its own grammar demanded), and `consolidate_values` composes the consolidation brief from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived LIVE - every row of that cluster or none, and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved refuses rather than briefing a session about a contradiction that no longer exists. `amendment_values` renders the accepted-cell before/after of the drifted rows `trace.reaccept_model` finds, BOUNDED by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes exactly as the first-acceptance mint does: a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree, a scope none of whose rows still differs from its accepted copy refuses naming them, and a row hanging under several SRs is rendered once, naming each chain; a scoped row that the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal for the sitting to rule, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a drifted row's tier, and its identity the digest of its recorded accepted text; a scope token naming a row that neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses the composition, naming the token; and a scoped row present live with no accepted-cell drift drops out as before; its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's snapshot copy (`baseline_snapshot.stamp` given the registry), never the newest write anywhere in the snapshot directory. For need, assumption and surrogate scopes, _unchained_amended_rows renders their drifted accepted cells through tier_owing, and the amendment aftermath takes its held-rung arm from adjudication_action. QUESTION HOME. `tier_questions` reads `prompts/spine-questions.md` at render time for the `{questions}` slot that `first_acceptance_values` and `amendment_values` return. Each `## ` section's next line declares its served tiers as `<!-- tiers: ... -->`, with `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, every-tier sections and sections serving the rows judged: `_judged_tiers` derives first-acceptance tiers from its `--accepts` registry walk, and amendment tiers from the rows shown. `combined_values` composes each section through `compose`, so its first-acceptance and amendment sections carry their own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section without a tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief naming that home; there is no fallback. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives neither an acceptance-registry token nor its per-token row map, and amendment aftermath identifies each verdict mark for a later turn. A CLARITY verdict is later re-anchored, naming its verdict on a held rung; a released-rung MEANING verdict it blesses is marked `MEANING+BLESS`; a held MEANING verdict is recommended to the operator.
  - after: The session route reads an adjudication row's `Brief` and `Adjudicates` cells through the same reading the merge slot uses, so a spelling or scope one accepts cannot move the other's bound. The row's declared `Brief` cell selects the brief's template: the trunk mint writes it at every adjudication it mints with a brief to declare, its report-less cancellation and clean-close spot-check rows declare none, and a row declaring an unknown brief, the retired conflict brief included, refuses. The brief is filled under the prompt loader's strict check, which refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared one, and both of the loop's routing arms reach it by one path, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Every shipped brief, the Done-when and combined briefs included, is composed whole by its own evidence derivation or refused with a reason, never in part. A refusal is a hold, never a fallback: the loop prints the reason on its stop banner and exits asking for an operator, running no session, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. A session handed a judging brief completes only when it commits, under the row's result trailer, the typed verdict line the brief demands at the path the brief names; no verdict, a missing machine line, a label outside the brief's closed set or a missing counter leaves it incomplete. The disposition brief follows the row's `SpecRef` to the closed spec, its immutable per-close report and that report's typed commit range, and lists the range's commits and changed paths clipped at a declared 80 lines; it refuses a clean close, which writes no report, and a report with no typed commit range. The evidence-gap brief re-runs the evidence-gap census rather than remembering it, joins each finding to its test case's method, expected and evidence cells and to the requirement or design text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean, a test case missing a listed cell and a target with no normative text. The consolidation brief is composed from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived live, every row of it or none; it refuses a row with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. The amendment brief is bounded by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes, as the first-acceptance mint does; a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree. For each scoped row that claims acceptance and differs from its recorded copy in an accepted cell or an adjudicated link (LLR-484), it shows each moved accepted cell and each moved adjudicated link by its cell name with its before and after, a link marked as one, so a row whose only moved cells are adjudicated links is shown; a row under several requirements is shown once, naming each chain. It asks of each link whether the row's text states what the link now points at. A moved trace cell no adjudicator judges is not shown. A scoped row the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a shown row's tier. Need, assumption and surrogate rows in scope are shown with their moved accepted cells by the one owing rule. A scope token naming a row neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses, naming the token; a scope none of whose rows has anything to judge refuses, naming the scope; and each other scoped row with nothing to judge is named as not shown. Its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's recorded copy, not the newest write anywhere in the record; it states one judged-text identity line per row shown (LLR-359); and its aftermath takes each verdict's held-rung arm from the one home of what a verdict owes on a held rung. The questions slot of the first-acceptance and amendment briefs is read at render time from the shipped questions home, one section per heading, each declaring on its next line the tiers it serves, `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, the sections serving every tier and those serving a judged tier: a first acceptance's tiers are those of the registries its rows to accept sit in, an amendment's those of the rows shown, and a combined sitting composes each section as its own brief, so each carries its own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section with no tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief, naming the home; there is no fallback, and no prompt template or copy of the spine-authoring skill restates a question of it. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives no registry argument for the act nor the rows each would cover, and the amendment aftermath names, for a later act turn, the mark each verdict owes: a CLARITY row is re-anchored later, naming its verdict on a held rung; a MEANING row whose new text the adjudicator would bless on a released rung is marked MEANING+BLESS; a held MEANING row is recommended to the operator.
- **Rationale**
  - before: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-OPERATOR` fold (LLR-161).
  - after: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived, and the one claim shown, the lane's report, is the template's own labelled subject. And a half-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than the reference cell's shape, because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap finding both cite the test-case registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a title substring, the fold LLR-161 retired. The amendment brief shows a moved adjudicated link beside the accepted cells because an act naming the row records it whole from live (LLR-245): a link the brief did not show would enter the record under a verdict that never saw it, and a scoped row left out unnamed lets a verdict and its scope disagree about what was judged.

### LLR LLR-313
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. render_review strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; render_critique strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. review_refusal accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form `VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>`, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. round_path chooses the next ordinal lane round filename, file_review writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and lane_commits resolves commits on a branch and requires the base to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A first plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when alone, opens with that commit, and refuses a missing rubric, plan or specification, a missing or beyond delta, a coverage failure, or a third round. A second plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when and the first critique's finding identifiers, never their text, uses convergence stance, and refuses a revision that leaves a first-critique finding neither covered nor excluded. A single-plan brief strictly fills the shipped single-plan template with the specification, ordered Done-when clauses, required registry references, and one planner output path; a revision additionally labels the committed plan and latest filed critique findings as claims under judgement. It refuses a missing specification, Done-when, plan or critique, or an unfilled slot. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. At the cap, a dispute findings file is written only from the committed second critique, giving each blocking and major finding the identifier of its position among all critique findings, its text verbatim, and a declared position; it refuses another identifier set or an existing file. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. A plan critique ties every severity class to a normal-use-reachable defect: a fail-open, data-loss, or security path counts only when a supported workflow or the kit's own routes reach it, and a rare or contrived path is MINOR at most or dismissed. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar.
  - after: A review round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. The review render strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; the critique render strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. Filing accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. A filed round takes the lane's next ordinal round file name, filing writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and the lane's commits are resolved on a branch, its base required to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A first plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when alone, opens with that commit, and refuses a missing rubric, plan or specification, a missing or beyond delta, a coverage failure, or a third round. A second plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when and the first critique's finding identifiers, never their text, uses convergence stance, and refuses a revision that leaves a first-critique finding neither covered nor excluded. A single-plan brief strictly fills the shipped single-plan template with the specification, ordered Done-when clauses, required registry references, and one planner output path; a revision additionally labels the committed plan and latest filed critique findings as claims under judgement. It refuses a missing specification, Done-when, plan or critique, or an unfilled slot. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. At the cap, a dispute findings file is written only from the committed second critique, giving each blocking and major finding the identifier of its position among all critique findings, its text verbatim, and a declared position; it refuses another identifier set or an existing file. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. A plan critique ties every severity class to a normal-use-reachable defect: a fail-open, data-loss, or security path counts only when a supported workflow or the kit's own routes reach it, and a rare or contrived path is MINOR at most or dismissed. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar. The command exits 0 when it writes a brief or files a review, a critique, a dispute findings file or a pass record, and 2 when it refuses, a refusal writing no requested brief or record.

### TC TC-161
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Expected**
  - before: Satisfies SR-146 AcceptanceCriteria: judging briefs order no act and identify the later act turn.
  - after: Each brief is handed to the session its row declares, with its derived evidence and every slot filled; each refusal returns its reason, composes no part of a brief and holds the row for an operator with no session run; the discriminator, the routing and each verdict grammar hold both ways; the amendment brief shows every moved accepted cell and adjudicated link of its scoped rows, marks the links and names each scoped row it does not show; the questions come from their one home; and no judging brief orders an act.
- **Method**
  - before: Drive the real loop against a fake agent CLI over throwaway git repos for the disposition, evidence-gap and amendment briefs, and capture what the session was actually handed: the brief its row declares rather than the worker assignment. Assert the session writes the typed verdict line the brief demands to the path the brief named and commits it under the row's result trailer, ending the run done; that a session committing the trailer with no verdict, or with a label outside the brief's closed enum, does not complete; that a row whose declared brief cannot be filled is held for an operator, with no session run, the reason printed and nothing committed under the row; and that a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. Compose each routed brief in process and assert each slot carries its real derivation: for the disposition brief, the lane's report verbatim, the closed spec and the commit facts; for the evidence-gap brief, the live census joined to each TC row's Method, Expected and Evidence and to the obligation its targets name; for the consolidation brief, the whole cluster, the other open rows, the cited SR and LLR text (a stated literal when the cluster cites none), the overlap findings, the recorded and current digest pairs and what earlier consolidations absorbed; for the first-acceptance brief, the whole chain of each SR holding a scoped row, the scoped `Drafted` rows marked as the question, a held or out-of-scope row labelled as not this session's and contributing no registry, an unrelated chain left out, no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU', no Status flip, and the later act turn named; TC-368 verifies act-side command quoting and registry scope, and TC-321 verifies act-side completion. Assert no `{slot}` survives in the composed disposition, evidence-gap, amendment and first-acceptance text. Then drive the refusals, each returning its reason and never a partial brief: a clean close with no per-close report, a report with no typed `commit_range`, a census that has come clean, a TC row missing a listed cell, a target with no normative text; a first-acceptance, amendment or consolidation row declaring no `Adjudicates` scope, a first acceptance whose scoped rows are all settled, gone from the spine or held for the operator, and an amendment none of whose scoped rows still differs from its accepted copy; a consolidation with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued, and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. Pin the discriminator: two rows with an identical SpecRef and different declared `Brief` cells compose differently. Pin the routing both ways: the routed set equals the shipped set and each routed brief names a shipped template, every brief a mint declares is shipped and every routed brief is declared by a mint, a row declaring `conflict`, which the kit no longer ships, refuses as an unknown brief, and an absent or unknown brief refuses. Pin each brief's typed verdict grammar: a well-formed line is accepted, and an absent file, a missing machine line, an out-of-enum label or a missing counter is refused with its reason. Pin the amendment arm's scope: the amendment mint writes the rows it routes as the row's `Adjudicates` scope, and a one-row mint's brief renders that row alone while another row's unadjudicated drift stays out of it, and a row hanging under two SRs is rendered once. The amendment arm covers two seams: IF-124, the `last_accepted` baseline read - the brief carries the snapshot as its anchor, naming for each registry shown the commit that last wrote that registry's copy rather than the newest write anywhere in the snapshot, only accepted cells reach the judge, and with no snapshot the brief holds and says FIRST ACCEPTANCE rather than fabricating an anchor; and IF-075, the re-acceptance model its before/after listing is read from. The amendment arm also renders a need, assumption or surrogate row in its scope with its drifted accepted cells before and after, and leaves out a drifted row of those tiers that the scope does not name. A scoped row removed from the live registry while its recorded copy holds it accepted is briefed as a removal, in a chained tier and in an unchained tier, for the single-kind amendment and combined classes; a present undrifted scoped row still drops beside it, and a scope token naming a row in neither home refuses, naming it. test_no_judging_brief_orders_an_act composes the first-acceptance, released and held amendment, and combined briefs and finds no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU' and no Status flip, while naming the later act turn. Compose a first-acceptance brief over an LLR and amendment briefs over an SR and an SN. Assert each carries the every-row questions and its own tier section, but no other tier section, and that a combined first-acceptance section carries its rows' questions. Point the renderer at a substitute home and assert that its text is carried, a change changes the brief, and an absent home, directory, undecodable file, section with no tiers line, or home serving none of the judged tiers refuses naming that home. Assert no question opening appears in any prompt template or spine-authoring skill copy.
  - after: Drive the real loop against a fake agent command over throwaway git repositories for the disposition, evidence-gap and amendment briefs, and capture what the session was handed: the brief its row declares, not the worker assignment. Assert the session writes the typed verdict line its brief demands to the path the brief named and commits it under the row's result trailer, ending the run done; that a session committing the trailer with no verdict, or with a label outside the brief's closed set, does not complete; that a row whose declared brief cannot be filled is held for an operator, no session run, the reason printed and nothing committed under the row; and that a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. Compose each routed brief in process and assert each slot carries its real derivation: for the disposition brief, the lane's report verbatim, the closed spec and the commit facts; for the evidence-gap brief, the live census joined to each test case's method, expected and evidence cells and to the obligation its targets name; for the consolidation brief, the whole cluster, the other open rows, the cited requirement and design text (a stated literal when the cluster cites none), the overlap findings, the recorded and current digest pairs and what earlier consolidations absorbed; for the first-acceptance brief, the whole chain of each requirement holding a scoped row, the scoped Drafted rows marked as the question, a held or out-of-scope row labelled not this session's and contributing no registry, an unrelated chain left out, no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU', no Status flip, and the later act turn named; TC-368 verifies act-side command quoting and registry scope, and TC-321 act-side completion. Assert no unfilled slot survives in the composed disposition, evidence-gap, amendment and first-acceptance text. Drive the refusals, each returning its reason and never part of a brief: a clean close with no per-close report, a report with no typed commit range, a census that has come clean, a test case missing a listed cell, a target with no normative text; a first-acceptance, amendment or consolidation row declaring no `Adjudicates` scope, a first acceptance whose scoped rows are all settled, gone from the spine or held for the operator, and an amendment none of whose scoped rows has a moved accepted cell or adjudicated link; a consolidation with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. Pin the discriminator: two rows with one `SpecRef` and different declared `Brief` cells compose differently. Pin the routing both ways: the routed briefs are the shipped ones and each names a shipped template, every brief a mint declares is shipped and every routed brief is declared by a mint, a row declaring the retired conflict brief refuses as unknown, and an absent or unknown brief refuses. Pin each brief's typed verdict grammar: a well-formed line is accepted, and an absent file, a missing machine line, an out-of-set label or a missing counter is refused with its reason. Pin the amendment brief: the amendment mint writes the rows it routes as the row's scope, a one-row mint's brief renders that row alone while another row's unjudged drift stays out, and a row under two requirements is rendered once; the brief carries the recorded copy as its anchor, naming for each registry shown the commit that last wrote its copy, and with no recorded copy it holds and says first acceptance. Over a row whose accepted cell alone moved, a row whose adjudicated link alone moved and a row with both, the brief shows each moved cell and link by its cell name with before and after, the link marked and the link-only row shown, and asks of a link whether the row's text states what it now points at; a moved trace cell no adjudicator judges does not reach it; a scoped row with nothing to judge is named as not shown; a link-only row's verdict line reads as any row's. It renders a need, assumption or surrogate row in its scope with its moved accepted cells, and leaves out a moved row of those tiers the scope does not name. A scoped row removed from the live registry while its recorded copy holds it accepted is briefed as a removal, in a chained and an unchained tier, for the single-kind and combined briefs, a present scoped row with nothing to judge being named beside it; a scope token naming a row in neither home refuses, naming it; and a combined sitting's amendment section shows a link-only row. Compose the first-acceptance, released and held amendment, and combined briefs and find no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU' and no Status flip, the later act turn named. Compose a first-acceptance brief over a design row and amendment briefs over a requirement and a need: each carries the every-row questions and its own tier's section and no other, and a combined first-acceptance section carries its rows' questions. Point the renderer at a substitute questions home: its text is carried, a change to it changes the brief, and an absent home, a directory, an undecodable file, a section with no tiers line, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses naming the home. Assert no question opening appears in any prompt template or any copy of the spine-authoring skill.

## SR-148 — Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall select exactly one work item for each assignment an unattended run admits from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone, in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, operator holds applied only from the declared acceptance level, nothing admitted past an operator-held stop, no prose surface and no predefined track in the derivation, no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface shipped for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.

> **Rationale.** Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. ONE SR PER (need, property): the loop's work-selection invariant — what the next work derives from, in what order, and that no hand-curated pointer surface participates in it — is stated here and nowhere else, so no second row can drift from it. An explicit three-way partition was rejected: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. The no-pointer obligation is stated for a migrated repository as well as a fresh scaffold, and the migrated half is the one that matters most: a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep, while an upgraded repository can retain the retired authority files and still pass. Fan-out re-stamp: this row states one decision — what an unattended run's next work derives from and in what order — and its children are the successive stages of that one selection, not separable contracts; the width is the deliberate consolidation of three rows that stated the same invariant into one, so re-splitting to satisfy the bound would restore the duplication the consolidation removed.


### LLR LLR-058
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Derives the dependency-ready frontier from the WI registry, the states of the open items its rows cite and the dispatcher reservations, never from prose; excludes terminally closed, deferred, reserved, protected and exclusive-conflicting work with reason codes; and exposes ready --explain, --format json and simulate --jobs N. The ready set contains exactly the work items whose hard work-item predecessors are done and whose OI-### edges name only items present in the open-items registry and not pending. A lane stopped early leaves the frontier through its terminal partial move.
  - after: Derives the dependency-ready frontier from the work-item registry, the states of the open items its rows cite and the dispatcher reservations, never from prose; excludes terminally closed, deferred, reserved, protected and exclusive-conflicting work with reason codes; and exposes it as a command that prints the frontier, or with its explanation every work item with its disposition and reason codes, and a simulated schedule over a given number of workers, as text lines (one per record, one per round) or as JSON (an array of the per-item records, an object carrying the rounds), refusing a worker count below one as a usage error. The ready set contains exactly the work items whose hard work-item predecessors are done and whose open-item edges name only items present in the open-items registry and not pending. A lane stopped early leaves the frontier through its terminal partial move. The status page's generated frontier block is drawn from this derivation, in its order, and a generator that finds no scheduler renders no frontier block rather than failing.
_adjudicated link — goes to a sitting_
- **Constrains**
  - before: (empty)
  - after: IF-071;IF-085;IF-171;IF-172

### LLR LLR-167
- **Detail**
  - before: The route reads `Brief` and `Adjudicates` through the same normalization the merge uses, so a spelling or scope accepted by one cannot change the other’s bound. The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint that has a brief to declare; the report-less cancellation and clean-close spot-check arms declare none); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `evidence_gap_values` RE-RUNS `census.evidence_gap_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `census.parse_evidence_gap` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal is a HOLD, never a fallback: `session_body` returns the reason and `agent_loop.route_session` prints it on the stop banner and returns `EXIT_NEEDS_OPERATOR` with no session run, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. EVERY shipped brief is routed through an assembler, including done-when and combined: `conflict` was RETIRED rather than filled (it had a template and a verdict grammar and never a mint, an assembler or a reader for the `needs=` its own grammar demanded), and `consolidate_values` composes the consolidation brief from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived LIVE - every row of that cluster or none, and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved refuses rather than briefing a session about a contradiction that no longer exists. `amendment_values` renders the accepted-cell before/after of the drifted rows `trace.reaccept_model` finds, BOUNDED by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes exactly as the first-acceptance mint does: a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree, a scope none of whose rows still differs from its accepted copy refuses naming them, and a row hanging under several SRs is rendered once, naming each chain; a scoped row that the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal for the sitting to rule, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a drifted row's tier, and its identity the digest of its recorded accepted text; a scope token naming a row that neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses the composition, naming the token; and a scoped row present live with no accepted-cell drift drops out as before; its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's snapshot copy (`baseline_snapshot.stamp` given the registry), never the newest write anywhere in the snapshot directory. For need, assumption and surrogate scopes, _unchained_amended_rows renders their drifted accepted cells through tier_owing, and the amendment aftermath takes its held-rung arm from adjudication_action. QUESTION HOME. `tier_questions` reads `prompts/spine-questions.md` at render time for the `{questions}` slot that `first_acceptance_values` and `amendment_values` return. Each `## ` section's next line declares its served tiers as `<!-- tiers: ... -->`, with `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, every-tier sections and sections serving the rows judged: `_judged_tiers` derives first-acceptance tiers from its `--accepts` registry walk, and amendment tiers from the rows shown. `combined_values` composes each section through `compose`, so its first-acceptance and amendment sections carry their own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section without a tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief naming that home; there is no fallback. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives neither an acceptance-registry token nor its per-token row map, and amendment aftermath identifies each verdict mark for a later turn. A CLARITY verdict is later re-anchored, naming its verdict on a held rung; a released-rung MEANING verdict it blesses is marked `MEANING+BLESS`; a held MEANING verdict is recommended to the operator.
  - after: The session route reads an adjudication row's `Brief` and `Adjudicates` cells through the same reading the merge slot uses, so a spelling or scope one accepts cannot move the other's bound. The row's declared `Brief` cell selects the brief's template: the trunk mint writes it at every adjudication it mints with a brief to declare, its report-less cancellation and clean-close spot-check rows declare none, and a row declaring an unknown brief, the retired conflict brief included, refuses. The brief is filled under the prompt loader's strict check, which refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared one, and both of the loop's routing arms reach it by one path, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Every shipped brief, the Done-when and combined briefs included, is composed whole by its own evidence derivation or refused with a reason, never in part. A refusal is a hold, never a fallback: the loop prints the reason on its stop banner and exits asking for an operator, running no session, so a judge is never briefed as a builder; only a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. A session handed a judging brief completes only when it commits, under the row's result trailer, the typed verdict line the brief demands at the path the brief names; no verdict, a missing machine line, a label outside the brief's closed set or a missing counter leaves it incomplete. The disposition brief follows the row's `SpecRef` to the closed spec, its immutable per-close report and that report's typed commit range, and lists the range's commits and changed paths clipped at a declared 80 lines; it refuses a clean close, which writes no report, and a report with no typed commit range. The evidence-gap brief re-runs the evidence-gap census rather than remembering it, joins each finding to its test case's method, expected and evidence cells and to the requirement or design text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean, a test case missing a listed cell and a target with no normative text. The consolidation brief is composed from the row's `Adjudicates` cluster re-derived live, every row of it or none; it refuses a row with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. The amendment brief is bounded by the row's `Adjudicates` scope, which the amendment mint writes from the rows it routes, as the first-acceptance mint does; a row declaring no scope refuses rather than reading the empty cell as the whole tree. For each scoped row that claims acceptance and differs from its recorded copy in an accepted cell or an adjudicated link (LLR-484), it shows each moved accepted cell and each moved adjudicated link by its cell name with its before and after, a link marked as one, so a row whose only moved cells are adjudicated links is shown; a row under several requirements is shown once, naming each chain. It asks of each link whether the row's text states what the link now points at. A moved trace cell no adjudicator judges is not shown. A scoped row the recorded copy holds accepted and the live registry no longer carries is briefed as a removal, in one block naming it removed, with its accepted cells as the before and the removal as the after, its tier counted for the questions, baseline and aftermath as a shown row's tier. Need, assumption and surrogate rows in scope are shown with their moved accepted cells by the one owing rule. A scope token naming a row neither the live registries nor the recorded copy holds refuses, naming the token; a scope none of whose rows has anything to judge refuses, naming the scope; and each other scoped row with nothing to judge is named as not shown. Its baseline line names, for each registry shown, the commit that last wrote that registry's recorded copy, not the newest write anywhere in the record; it states one judged-text identity line per row shown (LLR-359); and its aftermath takes each verdict's held-rung arm from the one home of what a verdict owes on a held rung. The questions slot of the first-acceptance and amendment briefs is read at render time from the shipped questions home, one section per heading, each declaring on its next line the tiers it serves, `*` serving every tier. It composes, in home order and without that declaration line, the sections serving every tier and those serving a judged tier: a first acceptance's tiers are those of the registries its rows to accept sit in, an amendment's those of the rows shown, and a combined sitting composes each section as its own brief, so each carries its own questions. An absent or unreadable home, a section with no tiers declaration, a home with no section, or a home serving none of the judged tiers refuses the brief, naming the home; there is no fallback, and no prompt template or copy of the spine-authoring skill restates a question of it. Judging briefs order no act: a first-acceptance brief derives no registry argument for the act nor the rows each would cover, and the amendment aftermath names, for a later act turn, the mark each verdict owes: a CLARITY row is re-anchored later, naming its verdict on a held rung; a MEANING row whose new text the adjudicator would bless on a released rung is marked MEANING+BLESS; a held MEANING row is recommended to the operator.
- **Rationale**
  - before: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-OPERATOR` fold (LLR-161).
  - after: Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived, and the one claim shown, the lane's report, is the template's own labelled subject. And a half-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than the reference cell's shape, because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and an evidence-gap finding both cite the test-case registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a title substring, the fold LLR-161 retired. The amendment brief shows a moved adjudicated link beside the accepted cells because an act naming the row records it whole from live (LLR-245): a link the brief did not show would enter the record under a verdict that never saw it, and a scoped row left out unnamed lets a verdict and its scope disagree about what was judged.

### LLR LLR-345
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A commit that changes an adoption record or moves a requesting specification into docs/work/complete or docs/archive/work/complete is admitted only when it is the close's own commit or carries an existing record with its request unchanged in some parent. A merge is judged only on the changes absent from every parent, under that same rule. The close's own commit has one readable parent, paired moves from one docs/work/active branch to docs/work/complete, no created or destroyed path outside those moves, the composed close subject, and each changed path outside docs/work exactly the inbound relink written by those moves as determined by the shared byte-exact relink reading; its record equals its request and every adopted id is valid in the parent tree. The tree under judgement is read strictly; a parent's specification whose front matter or record does not parse holds no record, while a parent git cannot read is refused and named. Every other record change, a carried record whose request changed or was removed, or a requesting move without a record is refused. The rule applies to the lane-commit walk and staged commit check, including their squash handling.
  - after: A commit that changes an adoption record or moves a requesting specification into the completed-work folder or its archived counterpart is admitted only when it is the close's own commit or carries an existing record with its request unchanged in some parent. A merge is judged only on the changes absent from every parent, under that same rule. The close's own commit has one readable parent, paired moves from one active claim folder to the completed-work folder, no created or destroyed path outside those moves, the composed close subject, and each changed path outside the work-item folders exactly the inbound relink written by those moves as determined by the shared byte-exact relink reading; its record equals its request and every adopted id is valid in the parent tree. The tree under judgement is read strictly; a parent's specification whose front matter or record does not parse holds no record, while a parent git cannot read is refused and named. Every other record change, a carried record whose request changed or was removed, or a requesting move without a record is refused. The rule applies to the lane-commit walk and staged commit check, including their squash handling. Run as the adoption-record step over the commit being made, the rule prints one step line, FAIL with each refusal or ok, and exits 1 when it refuses a record change and 0 otherwise.

  - after: IF-303;IF-304

### TC TC-161
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Expected**
  - before: Satisfies SR-146 AcceptanceCriteria: judging briefs order no act and identify the later act turn.
  - after: Each brief is handed to the session its row declares, with its derived evidence and every slot filled; each refusal returns its reason, composes no part of a brief and holds the row for an operator with no session run; the discriminator, the routing and each verdict grammar hold both ways; the amendment brief shows every moved accepted cell and adjudicated link of its scoped rows, marks the links and names each scoped row it does not show; the questions come from their one home; and no judging brief orders an act.
- **Method**
  - before: Drive the real loop against a fake agent CLI over throwaway git repos for the disposition, evidence-gap and amendment briefs, and capture what the session was actually handed: the brief its row declares rather than the worker assignment. Assert the session writes the typed verdict line the brief demands to the path the brief named and commits it under the row's result trailer, ending the run done; that a session committing the trailer with no verdict, or with a label outside the brief's closed enum, does not complete; that a row whose declared brief cannot be filled is held for an operator, with no session run, the reason printed and nothing committed under the row; and that a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. Compose each routed brief in process and assert each slot carries its real derivation: for the disposition brief, the lane's report verbatim, the closed spec and the commit facts; for the evidence-gap brief, the live census joined to each TC row's Method, Expected and Evidence and to the obligation its targets name; for the consolidation brief, the whole cluster, the other open rows, the cited SR and LLR text (a stated literal when the cluster cites none), the overlap findings, the recorded and current digest pairs and what earlier consolidations absorbed; for the first-acceptance brief, the whole chain of each SR holding a scoped row, the scoped `Drafted` rows marked as the question, a held or out-of-scope row labelled as not this session's and contributing no registry, an unrelated chain left out, no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU', no Status flip, and the later act turn named; TC-368 verifies act-side command quoting and registry scope, and TC-321 verifies act-side completion. Assert no `{slot}` survives in the composed disposition, evidence-gap, amendment and first-acceptance text. Then drive the refusals, each returning its reason and never a partial brief: a clean close with no per-close report, a report with no typed `commit_range`, a census that has come clean, a TC row missing a listed cell, a target with no normative text; a first-acceptance, amendment or consolidation row declaring no `Adjudicates` scope, a first acceptance whose scoped rows are all settled, gone from the spine or held for the operator, and an amendment none of whose scoped rows still differs from its accepted copy; a consolidation with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued, and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. Pin the discriminator: two rows with an identical SpecRef and different declared `Brief` cells compose differently. Pin the routing both ways: the routed set equals the shipped set and each routed brief names a shipped template, every brief a mint declares is shipped and every routed brief is declared by a mint, a row declaring `conflict`, which the kit no longer ships, refuses as an unknown brief, and an absent or unknown brief refuses. Pin each brief's typed verdict grammar: a well-formed line is accepted, and an absent file, a missing machine line, an out-of-enum label or a missing counter is refused with its reason. Pin the amendment arm's scope: the amendment mint writes the rows it routes as the row's `Adjudicates` scope, and a one-row mint's brief renders that row alone while another row's unadjudicated drift stays out of it, and a row hanging under two SRs is rendered once. The amendment arm covers two seams: IF-124, the `last_accepted` baseline read - the brief carries the snapshot as its anchor, naming for each registry shown the commit that last wrote that registry's copy rather than the newest write anywhere in the snapshot, only accepted cells reach the judge, and with no snapshot the brief holds and says FIRST ACCEPTANCE rather than fabricating an anchor; and IF-075, the re-acceptance model its before/after listing is read from. The amendment arm also renders a need, assumption or surrogate row in its scope with its drifted accepted cells before and after, and leaves out a drifted row of those tiers that the scope does not name. A scoped row removed from the live registry while its recorded copy holds it accepted is briefed as a removal, in a chained tier and in an unchained tier, for the single-kind amendment and combined classes; a present undrifted scoped row still drops beside it, and a scope token naming a row in neither home refuses, naming it. test_no_judging_brief_orders_an_act composes the first-acceptance, released and held amendment, and combined briefs and finds no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU' and no Status flip, while naming the later act turn. Compose a first-acceptance brief over an LLR and amendment briefs over an SR and an SN. Assert each carries the every-row questions and its own tier section, but no other tier section, and that a combined first-acceptance section carries its rows' questions. Point the renderer at a substitute home and assert that its text is carried, a change changes the brief, and an absent home, directory, undecodable file, section with no tiers line, or home serving none of the judged tiers refuses naming that home. Assert no question opening appears in any prompt template or spine-authoring skill copy.
  - after: Drive the real loop against a fake agent command over throwaway git repositories for the disposition, evidence-gap and amendment briefs, and capture what the session was handed: the brief its row declares, not the worker assignment. Assert the session writes the typed verdict line its brief demands to the path the brief named and commits it under the row's result trailer, ending the run done; that a session committing the trailer with no verdict, or with a label outside the brief's closed set, does not complete; that a row whose declared brief cannot be filled is held for an operator, no session run, the reason printed and nothing committed under the row; and that a row declaring no brief builds from the worker assignment. Drive the refusals, each returning its reason and never part of a brief: a clean close with no per-close report, a report with no typed commit range, a census that has come clean, a test case missing a listed cell, a target with no normative text; a first-acceptance, amendment or consolidation row declaring no `Adjudicates` scope, a first acceptance whose scoped rows are all settled, gone from the spine or held for the operator, and an amendment none of whose scoped rows has a moved accepted cell or adjudicated link; a consolidation with no `Digests` cell, a cluster row no longer queued and a cluster whose overlap has dissolved. Pin the routing both ways: the routed briefs are the shipped ones and each names a shipped template, every brief a mint declares is shipped and every routed brief is declared by a mint, a row declaring the retired conflict brief refuses as unknown, and an absent or unknown brief refuses. It renders a need, assumption or surrogate row in its scope with its moved accepted cells, and leaves out a moved row of those tiers the scope does not name. Compose the first-acceptance, released and held amendment, and combined briefs and find no snapshot command, no 'BY YOU' and no Status flip, the later act turn named. ## SR-150 — Stakeholder-need cells stay in stakeholder language

> **Requirement.** The harness shall report each stakeholder-need `need` cell that contains an internal path, an implementation-only identifier or a process citation, naming the row and the offending phrase — at a severity that warns by default and fails only under --strict, exempting a name declared on a reviewed exception list that ships empty (a name that is itself a user-facing interface belongs in a need), and reporting nothing for acceptance or engineering-requirement cells, which SN-033 exempts by its own text.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-033 — its accepted acceptance commissions exactly this check, and until this row it did not exist. The need tier was measured clean when this landed (0 of 27 cells), and the row sits deliberately AHEAD of the SR re-tier's churn: locking the clean state in costs one warn-first step now; trusting a large re-statement pass not to dirty it costs an audit later. Warn-first is the ruled posture, not a softness — a form heuristic over accepted stakeholder prose must not gate without an operator ruling, and that ruling has not been made.


### LLR LLR-494 — ADDED since the snapshot, Drafted — never accepted
- **LLR-ID**: LLR-494
- **SR-Refs**: SR-150
- **Title**: The need-form check exits 0 at its default severity and, in its strict mode, 1 on anything it reports
- **Detail**: The need-form check exits 0 at its default severity, whatever it reports. In its strict mode it exits 1 when it reports a finding, an exception-list line its grammar cannot read, or a present registry with no need cell to scan, and 0 when its scan is clean.
- **Rationale**: The requirement fixes the severity, warning by default and failing only in the declared strict mode; the exit code is how the harness and a person's shell read it, so it is stated where the check's output is.
- **TestRefs**: TC-587
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 6
- **Constrains**: IF-121

### TC TC-587 — ADDED since the snapshot, Drafted — never accepted
- **TC-ID**: TC-587
- **Verifies**: SR-150;LLR-494
- **Level**: Integration
- **Method**: Run the need-form check at both severities over a need cell carrying offending phrases, over an exception list holding lines its grammar cannot read and then a well-formed one, and over a present registry with no need cell; run it at its default severity over the live registry and over a project with no needs registry.
- **Tier**: Full
- **Expected**: Every default-severity run exits 0, findings included; the strict run exits 1 on the offending phrases, on the unreadable exception lines and on the empty registry, and 0 once the exception list is well-formed and the scan is clean.
- **Automated**: Yes
- **Status**: Drafted
- **Phase**: 6
- **Evidence**: tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_a_malformed_allow_line_is_reported_by_name_and_line; tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_a_present_but_vacuous_registry_is_reported_not_clean; tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_an_absent_needs_registry_is_a_clean_skip; tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_dirty_need_cell_names_the_row_and_each_offending_phrase; tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_strict_promotes_the_same_findings_to_exit_1; tests/test_check_need_form.py::test_the_live_registry_is_clean_at_zero_findings

## SR-154 — Independent review routed across families

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall obtain, when unattended work reaches integration, each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared acceptance level, and, where a claimed work item is marked for an in-lane single plan, refuse build output at its making or landing until a pass record binds its plan and Done-when.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. The row also carries SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on. That half had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it. fan-out re-stamp: the single plan's independent critique is one obligation carried by its briefs and filing and by its gate; the routing children remain separate.


### LLR LLR-044
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Parses pair rows and enable tokens; resolves versions; merges Env; selects by tier, family, cooldown, and order with legacy Provider compatibility. Selection tiers up and never down, honors family preference with documented fallback, resolves a version-less token deterministically, and reports no available route honestly rather than guessing. The pair rows are docs/agents.toml's; the consent surface whose presence turns managed selection on is docs/agents-enabled. Each enable-list line is one entry; selection, preference and cooldowns carry that entry rather than its bare route id, and a registry row is read only through the entry's route. An unqualified entry key is its route id; a qualified entry key is ROUTE@ACCOUNT.
  - after: Parses the agent registry's pair rows and the enable list's tokens; resolves versions; merges a row's environment entries into its launch; and selects by tier, family, cooldown and order, a legacy provider column reading as the family. Selection tiers up and never down, honors family preference with documented fallback, resolves a version-less token deterministically, and reports no available route honestly rather than guessing. The pair rows are the agent registry's; the consent surface whose presence turns managed selection on is the enable list. Each enable-list line is one entry, in preference order; selection, preference and cooldowns carry that entry rather than its bare route id, and a registry row is read only through the entry's route. An unqualified entry key is its route id; a qualified entry key is the route id joined to an account id by an at sign.

### LLR LLR-046
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Computes advisory substance components and decay history; treats tripwires as gates and never rewards length. Severity hygiene rides the same non-scored gate path as the anti-gaming tripwires; corroboration feeds the component scores; the declared severity set makes a bullet a finding only when its bracket names one of its members, without regard to case, and another bracketed word is prose. Verdict parsing takes its word and declared count from the shared strict review-line reader, returning no verdict when that reader refuses, and scoreboard round trips are deterministic.
  - after: Computes advisory substance components and decay history; treats tripwires as gates and never rewards length. Severity hygiene rides the same non-scored gate path as the anti-gaming tripwires; corroboration feeds the component scores; the declared severity set makes a bullet a finding only when its bracket names one of its members, without regard to case, and another bracketed word is prose. Verdict parsing takes its word and declared count from the shared strict review-line reader, returning no verdict when that reader refuses, and scoreboard round trips are deterministic. A review file's verdict is its line reading VERDICT: then APPROVE or CHANGES-REQUESTED and findings= with the declared count, and a declared count that differs from the number of finding lines fires the count-mismatch tripwire. Recording a round reads the scoreboard's standing per-provider tallies, decays the tally of each provider the round scores by the declared factor before adding that provider's substance for the round, appends the round to the history and rewrites the scoreboard.

### LLR LLR-262
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The coordinator's session log records each session's phase and its exact commit range (`# commits: before..after`), and records a REVIEW session's full or narrow scope (`# review-scope: base..sha`); session_range and parse_range read the range, review_logs yields the gating span with it, and range_paths lists the paths the range changed with renames split. scope_offenders names every changed path other than the REVIEW session's own verdict file, the round file named for the session's train, ordinal and logged phase under any reviewed sha and tag; record paths are not exempt. Right after a REVIEW session the loop asks scope_offenders of the range it just took and, on any offender, stops the run needing an operator with the paths named; a session log is append-only evidence, so every reader takes each log as the commit that ADDED it recorded it (log_history, review_logs), never at the branch tip, and the merge ladder's review-scope rung first refuses by name any commit in the lane that modified or deleted a session log, then asks the same function of every REVIEW session log the branch committed (review_scope_refusal) and refuses the merge by name, an unreadable log or range refusing too. After a REVIEW or CRITIQUE session, uncommitted changes not present before it, other than the coordinator's own session log, fail the draw through the existing failed-draw path - the route cooled and the same phase re-drawn - with those paths stashed under one entry named for the session, so the re-drawn session starts clean; when that stash fails the run stops needing an operator with the paths and the failure named, and nothing is re-drawn. read_verdict reads the verdict blob as committed at HEAD in both arms, so an uncommitted verdict is never routed on; session_body binds an adjudication's composed request beside its verdict before the loop calls it; after the call, run_iteration records the loop call's success from call_succeeded in plan, and adjudication_bookkeeping calls record_outcome against those bound kinds and uses its returned why as the completion obligation: a failed call is recorded failed, keeps that obligation and does not read its verdict. logged_rounds reads each round at the end of its session's recorded range, and only when that range changed the round file: a later commit rewriting the file changes nothing the gate counts, a file its session never committed is no round, and a key whose logs record different phases or ranges yields none. done_when.items reads a spec's Done-when section, through kitlib.registry.done_when_section, as items: a list item with its continuation lines, or a prose paragraph, across subsections. changes compares a claim-time text with a later one after stripping ticks, strikethrough and whitespace; a later item carries a claim-time one when it equals it or extends it with evidence in the form closed specs use - appended text opening with an evidence separator (a dash, an arrow, a bracket, a bar or a check mark) and carrying an evidence token (a capitalised completion word from the closed set LANDED, DONE, MET, VERIFIED, SHIPPED, PASSED, FIXED and COVERED, a path or test id, a backticked name, or a commit sha) - while any other appended text, after closing punctuation or not, is a change; and every claim-time item not carried is reported as changed and every later item carrying none as added. The claim warns by name on stderr for each claimed row with no Done-when item and still claims. When a review round is composed, the brief gains a block quoting each change between the spec under active/<train>/ at the lane's base and the lane's own copy. At merge, intake evaluates each claimed non-adjudication row. An unreadable claim refuses the merge-time mint, naming why; the merge itself stands and recovery is a trunk-side fix. For an absent claim, intake emits its stderr line that the Done-when check did not run and mints nothing. For a readable claim whose closed Done-when changed against trunk's pre-merge copy under active/<branch>, intake proceeds through the existing arms: when no verdict line or operator ruling in the merged tree binds the closed text's digest, it mints one brief-bearing done-when adjudication row, titled per row and branch so a re-run dedupes, with Adjudicates naming the closed work item and Context quoting each change; when a covering SUCCESSOR verdict carries Dispositions drafts, it mints the Done-when part's own drafts instead - the whole single-kind verdict or its one ## done-when section - and refuses repeated Dispositions inside that valid section or one supplying no draft. A sitting with no or repeated done-when section, or a DONE-WHEN line under another heading, is invalid, covers nothing, and leaves the uncovered arm to mint the goalposts row. review_logs reads the resolved final-reviewer mode once per call through reviewer_dials and passes it to every REVIEW log's gating_span, so all readers apply one mode.
  - after: The coordinator's session log records each session's phase and its exact commit range (`# commits: before..after`), and records a review session's full or narrow scope (`# review-scope: base..sha`); the range is read back from the log, the review logs yield the gating span with it, and the paths the range changed are listed with renames split. The scope rule names every changed path other than the review session's own verdict file, the round file named for the session's train, ordinal and logged phase under any reviewed commit and tag; record paths are not exempt. Right after a review session the loop asks the scope rule of the range it just took and, on any offender, stops the run needing an operator with the paths named; a session log is append-only evidence, so every reader takes each log as the commit that added it recorded it, never at the branch tip, and the merge ladder's review-scope rung first refuses by name any commit in the lane that modified or deleted a session log, then asks the same rule of every review session log the branch committed and refuses the merge by name, an unreadable log or range refusing too. After a review or critique session, uncommitted changes not present before it, other than the coordinator's own session log, fail the draw through the existing failed-draw path - the route cooled and the same phase re-drawn - with those paths stashed under one entry named for the session, so the re-drawn session starts clean; when that stash fails the run stops needing an operator with the paths and the failure named, and nothing is re-drawn. The loop reads the verdict as committed at HEAD in both arms, so an uncommitted verdict is never routed on. The loop binds an adjudication's composed request beside its verdict before it calls it; after the call it records whether the call succeeded, and its bookkeeping records the call's outcome against those bound kinds and takes the reason that record returns as the completion obligation: a failed call is recorded failed, keeps that obligation and does not read its verdict. The logged rounds read each round at the end of its session's recorded range, and only when that range changed the round file: a later commit rewriting the file changes nothing the gate counts, a file its session never committed is no round, and a key whose logs record different phases or ranges yields none. A spec's Done-when section, read through the registry's one Done-when heading rule, is a list of items: a list item with its continuation lines, or a prose paragraph, across subsections. The comparison reads a claim-time text against a later one after stripping ticks, strikethrough and whitespace; a later item carries a claim-time one when it equals it or extends it with evidence in the form closed specs use - appended text opening with an evidence separator (a dash, an arrow, a bracket, a bar or a check mark) and carrying an evidence token (a capitalised completion word from the closed set LANDED, DONE, MET, VERIFIED, SHIPPED, PASSED, FIXED and COVERED, a path or test id, a backticked name, or a commit sha) - while any other appended text, after closing punctuation or not, is a change; and every claim-time item not carried is reported as changed and every later item carrying none as added. The claim warns by name on standard error for each claimed row with no Done-when item and still claims. When a review round is composed, the brief gains a block quoting each change between the spec in the lane's active directory at the lane's base and the lane's own copy. At merge, intake evaluates each claimed non-adjudication row. An unreadable claim refuses the merge-time mint, naming why; the merge itself stands and recovery is a trunk-side fix. For an absent claim, intake emits its standard-error line that the Done-when check did not run and mints nothing. For a readable claim whose closed Done-when changed against trunk's pre-merge copy in the branch's active directory, intake proceeds through the existing arms: when no verdict line or operator ruling in the merged tree binds the closed text's digest, it mints one brief-bearing done-when adjudication row, titled per row and branch so a re-run dedupes, with Adjudicates naming the closed work item and Context quoting each change; when a covering SUCCESSOR verdict carries Dispositions drafts, it mints the Done-when part's own drafts instead - the whole single-kind verdict or its one ## done-when section - and refuses repeated Dispositions inside that valid section or one supplying no draft. A sitting with no or repeated done-when section, or a DONE-WHEN line under another heading, is invalid, covers nothing, and leaves the uncovered arm to mint the goalposts row. The review logs read the resolved final-reviewer mode once per call and pass it to every review log's gating span, so all readers apply one mode.
