### LLR LLR-313
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. render_review strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; render_critique strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. review_refusal accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form `VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>`, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. round_path chooses the next ordinal lane round filename, file_review writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and lane_commits resolves commits on a branch and requires the base to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A first plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when alone, opens with that commit, and refuses a missing rubric, plan or specification, a missing or beyond delta, a coverage failure, or a third round. A second plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when and the first critique's finding identifiers, never their text, uses convergence stance, and refuses a revision that leaves a first-critique finding neither covered nor excluded. A single-plan brief strictly fills the shipped single-plan template with the specification, ordered Done-when clauses, required registry references, and one planner output path; a revision additionally labels the committed plan and latest filed critique findings as claims under judgement. It refuses a missing specification, Done-when, plan or critique, or an unfilled slot. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. At the cap, a dispute findings file is written only from the committed second critique, giving each blocking and major finding the identifier of its position among all critique findings, its text verbatim, and a declared position; it refuses another identifier set or an existing file. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. A plan critique ties every severity class to a normal-use-reachable defect: a fail-open, data-loss, or security path counts only when a supported workflow or the kit's own routes reach it, and a rare or contrived path is MINOR at most or dismissed. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar.
  - after: A review round accepts only a full-lane review with no prior findings or a narrow review that names the prior findings file. The review render strictly fills the shipped reviewer template with the work-item specification, lane range, scratch location, optional review rubric and verdict destination; an empty derived set is a valid round, renders no test command, and states that the row touches no code; listed evidence and reported gaps render beside the test command; the critique render strictly fills the shipped critique template with the work-item specification and named rubric. Both refuse a missing specification, unreadable or empty named input, or unfilled template slot before a brief is written. Filing accepts only a verdict bound to the reviewed full commit after the shared review-line reader accepts exactly one line whose keyword, in any case, is VERDICT, in exactly the canonical form VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>, whose count matches the finding lines the review scorer reads from declared-severity brackets and whose changes request names a finding. A filed round takes the lane's next ordinal round file name, filing writes only a recorded verdict as that round record when the round reader reads its lane path back as that lane's scope, and the lane's commits are resolved on a branch, its base required to be an ancestor of the reviewed commit. A first plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when alone, opens with that commit, and refuses a missing rubric, plan or specification, a missing or beyond delta, a coverage failure, or a third round. A second plan critique is bound to its named lane commit, includes coverage over the Done-when and the first critique's finding identifiers, never their text, uses convergence stance, and refuses a revision that leaves a first-critique finding neither covered nor excluded. A single-plan brief strictly fills the shipped single-plan template with the specification, ordered Done-when clauses, required registry references, and one planner output path; a revision additionally labels the committed plan and latest filed critique findings as claims under judgement. It refuses a missing specification, Done-when, plan or critique, or an unfilled slot. A critique record is filed only when it is bound to an ancestor that holds the plan and has the verdict form; a pass record is written only from committed cited records, passing plan coverage, and a passing delta, after an acceptance or an accepted dismissal of every blocking and major second-round finding. At the cap, a dispute findings file is written only from the committed second critique, giving each blocking and major finding the identifier of its position among all critique findings, its text verbatim, and a declared position; it refuses another identifier set or an existing file. The second plan critique's brief never carries the first critique's verdict or any finding's text. A review round is ordinary by default or convergence: ordinary emits no stance line, while convergence emits one fixed stance line after the range and refuses a narrow scope or unknown stance before writing a brief. An ordinary review reading scope reads the delegated-decisions record and filed plan critiques, while a convergence scope excludes both; a convergence render refuses a reviewer template whose reading-scope command does not carry the review-record exclusion exactly once. The scope critique names the component and interface registries, derived architecture, runtime flows, and dependency ledger, marking an absent registry; the single-plan brief and scope critique render their registry lines from one declared set. A plan critique ties every severity class to a normal-use-reachable defect: a fail-open, data-loss, or security path counts only when a supported workflow or the kit's own routes reach it, and a rare or contrived path is MINOR at most or dismissed. Its confirming round treats a new finding as BLOCKER or MAJOR only when it is a reachable fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when gap; anything else is MINOR. A finding line bearing the convergence-class tag defined by LLR-311 is filed and counted as one finding under the unchanged verdict grammar. The command exits 0 when it writes a brief or files a review, a critique, a dispute findings file or a pass record, and 2 when it refuses, a refusal writing no requested brief or record.

### LLR LLR-337
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The gate reads only rows claimed by the lane's own active folder at the claim base. The closed planmode set is absent, empty, none, single and dual: absent, empty, none and dual are not gated; single is gated; every other normalized value, and a value that is not a string, is held by name. The closed pre-record change set is the record homes and a close: plan records, review records, decision records, session logs, log fragments, the claimed row's own specification, that specification's move to a closed folder with its report and byte-exact inbound-link redirects, or, as a merge's own changes, the compiled change log and the views the trunk regeneration list names; every other own change is build output. Build output is refused unless a parent holds a pass record binding the row, plan digest and Done-when digest left by the change. A changed pass record is accepted only against its first parent when its nine-key grammar is complete, its delta is none or within and is the plan's own line, its digests match the parent's plan and Done-when, its cited critique is byte-identical in that parent, bound to its reviewed commit and accepted by the verdict grammar, and its dispute pair is both present or both empty; a cited critique record is only round one or round two, and any other round is no record. A second-round non-approval additionally requires an accepted dispute dismissal of every blocking and major finding. At filing and commit creation, the reviewed tree also holds the bound plan bytes and Done-when digest. The gate judges the staged lane change, squash-carried lane commits, and landing lane commits oldest first; a squashed tip that no local branch contains is held by name while the landing tree carries any single or out-of-set claim copy; it compares a merge with every parent and holds unreadable claim copies, diffs, listings, records or parents. Trunk and a lane with no single row are not gated. The Done-when digest a record binds is unmoved by a tick or evidence on a Done-when item, so neither unbinds the record. With the trunk undeclared, a branch whose HEAD holds no claim folder is judged at HEAD; any other branch is refused naming the declaration.
  - after: The gate reads only rows claimed by the lane's own active folder at the claim base. The closed planmode set is absent, empty, none, single and dual: absent, empty, none and dual are not gated; single is gated; every other normalized value, and a value that is not a string, is held by name. The closed pre-record change set is the record homes and a close: plan records, review records, decision records, session logs, log fragments, the claimed row's own specification, that specification's move to a closed folder with its report and byte-exact inbound-link redirects, or, as a merge's own changes, the compiled change log and the views the trunk regeneration list names; every other own change is build output. Build output is refused unless a parent holds a pass record binding the row, plan digest and Done-when digest left by the change. A changed pass record is accepted only against its first parent when its nine-key grammar is complete, its delta is none or within and is the plan's own line, its digests match the parent's plan and Done-when, its cited critique is byte-identical in that parent, bound to its reviewed commit and accepted by the verdict grammar, and its dispute pair is both present or both empty; a cited critique record is only round one or round two, and any other round is no record. A second-round non-approval additionally requires an accepted dispute dismissal of every blocking and major finding. At filing and commit creation, the reviewed tree also holds the bound plan bytes and Done-when digest. The gate judges the staged lane change, squash-carried lane commits, and landing lane commits oldest first; a squashed tip that no local branch contains is held by name while the landing tree carries any single or out-of-set claim copy; it compares a merge with every parent and holds unreadable claim copies, diffs, listings, records or parents. Trunk and a lane with no single row are not gated. The Done-when digest a record binds is unmoved by a tick or evidence on a Done-when item, so neither unbinds the record. With the trunk undeclared, a branch whose HEAD holds no claim folder is judged at HEAD; any other branch is refused naming the declaration. Run as the plan-gate step over the commit being made, the gate prints one step line, FAIL with each hold or ok, and exits 1 when a commit is held and 0 otherwise.

## SR-156 — Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landing

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall run exactly one work item per lane on bounded, mutually isolated lanes and narrow every lane back to the integration branch through one serial, fail-closed seam that runs the declared checks on the composed tree — parking a failing candidate and stopping the queue loudly, draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and reconstructing claim and queue state from version-control history alone at any crash boundary, never a half-integrated authoritative state.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-027 WHOLE — ready work fans out across bounded parallel lanes and that fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed) and SN-025 (recovery from version-control history alone). ONE DECISION PER ROW: "serial integration is one delivered contract" is sound for the SEAM, but lane execution, claim authority and the trunk-side mint of work-item identity are not the seam, and a row stating the seam alone leaves SN-027's FIRST half — bounded lanes, isolated working trees, a worker ceiling — stated nowhere. This row therefore states the whole lane LIFECYCLE (claim, run isolated, close, land), which is what closes that hole; the trunk-only authority contract sheds to SR-170. The two fail independently: a perfectly serialized landing that lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies this row and not that one. Terminal close is SR-144's and is not restated here — one SR per (need, property), and both rows sit under SN-027 — so what this row carries of it is only that a terminal branch still lands through the one serial seam.

The trunk commit goes through bookkeeping.commit, under that helper's contract (IF-186) for isolation and install, scoped to the claim's own writes - each spec's link-aware move into active/<branch>/ and every file the move relinks, plus the regeneration's writes: a dirty path in that scope refuses by name before any step or checkout write, and the branch is cut before trunk advances. finished_branches: a claimed branch whose tip holds no spec under active/<branch>/ (the closing move IS the signal). integrate_one: --no-ff --no-commit merge, trunk_step.py folded into the merge commit so it passes its own floor, check.py --jobs 0 at the declared tier on the composed tree read fail-closed (nonzero exit OR any SKIP line refuses), verdict gate per docs/process.toml [policies] review_rounds computed over the branch's own round files, restricted to a fresh full-lane round in either mode, or a final round only where reviewer retention is on and final_reviewer is persisted, whose scope runs from the lane's recorded claim base to the reviewed commit; a narrow round and a retained full round never admit. The gate is governed by TREE IDENTITY: a verdict counts only while it names the branch's current non-record tree at the work tip, so there is no ordering rule and no time comparison; an unreadable claim commit holds by name, a narrow-only lane never clears, and a phase whose latest round is invalid evidence holds by name as having no valid verdict; whether an adjudication lane owes a verdict at all is docs/process.toml [authority] adjudication_review, read through the one function the loop's round scheduler also reads - over the same input, since which copy of a branch's spec answers for it when both the terminal and the active home carry one is decided by a single declared precedence (terminal first: it is what the closing session did) that both callers ask for rather than each walking the homes in its own order. The gate is governed by tree identity: a verdict counts only while it names the branch's current non-record tree at the work tip, so there is no ordering rule and no time comparison; an unreadable claim commit holds by name, a narrow-only lane never clears, and a phase whose latest round is invalid evidence holds by name as having no valid verdict. Whether an adjudication lane owes a verdict at all is the process configuration's adjudication-review dial, read through the one function the loop's round scheduler also reads, over the same input, since which copy of a branch's spec answers for it when both the terminal and the active home carry one is decided by a single declared precedence (terminal first: it is what the closing session did) that both callers ask for rather than each walking the homes in its own order. The trailer is a cross-check over the newest trailer at the tree under judgement, refusing on a differing verdict word or a round count above the evidence and never on one below it - the trailer's writer is best-effort, so a stamp the evidence outruns is a missed stamp rather than a forgery, and understating cannot buy a merge the round files do not already buy. A passing branch advances trunk fast-forward only and is deleted; a failing candidate is parked. The audit reads the trunk's first-parent, non-merge commits since the window's base revision, and a path outside the work registry, the log fragments, the log and the stack profile's declared generated set flags its commit. A missing stack profile or an empty product test declaration refuses the merge; failing checks park the candidate and stop the queue rather than composing past it. The shared commit-floor refusal precedes even that first guard refusal and the dispatch lock. The integrator's command line runs one command against the repository root it is given, or the working directory: a claim (one work-item id and a branch), a refresh (a branch, at a check tier), record checks (a branch and the commit check set), integrate (at a check tier), the Done-when hold, or the audit (the window's base revision); each of them, refusing, prints the reason and exits nonzero.

### LLR LLR-151
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The §A4.1 authority flip's integrator half (docs/concurrency-v2.md §A4.1): _claim_refusal's safety_class != ordinary arm is DELETED (admission is the dispatcher's decision) and the claim REQUIRES the dispatch lock (agent_common.dispatch_lock_path, out/agent-loop.lock - the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime), acquired at ONE site (_dispatch_lock, mirroring _slot's one-site discipline for the merge slot's own lock) with the ladder run before it (the ladder carries no clean-trunk rung: the claim commits through bookkeeping.commit, which refuses by name a dirty path the claim must write and never stages the lock's own file): a hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim passes dispatch_lock_held=True. a claim commit moves one queued spec; empty or multi-item input refuses before any move, commit, branch or worktree; historical multi-item claim commits stay readable and are neither split nor re-claimed, and integrate() takes an optional branches= restriction so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes.
  - after: The integrator's half of the dispatcher's authority flip: the claim no longer refuses a work item whose safety class is not ordinary (admission is the dispatcher's decision), and the claim requires the dispatch lock, the lock a live dispatcher holds for its process lifetime, acquired at one site, as the merge slot's own lock is, with the refusal ladder run before it. The ladder carries no clean-trunk rung: the claim commits through the shared bookkeeping commit, which refuses by name a dirty path the claim must write and never stages the lock's own file. A hand claim on an idle station works, a hand claim during live lanes is unrepresentable, and the dispatcher's in-process claim declares that it already holds the lock. A claim commit moves one queued spec; empty or multi-item input refuses before any move, commit, branch or worktree; historical multi-item claim commits stay readable and are neither split nor re-claimed; and the merge queue takes an optional restriction to named branches, so the dispatcher merges each lane's branch as its own refresh completes. The shared bookkeeping commit, the one trunk-side write the claim and the intake mint both make, plans the paths its step may write over the committed tree, so an uncommitted edit neither narrows nor widens that plan, and refuses by name a dirty path inside it before writing anything. It performs the step and the trunk step's regeneration in a scratch worktree cut from the committed tree, running the regeneration's committed copy wherever the repository carries the kit, so no uncommitted edit in the checkout reaches or shapes what it commits, and it commits exactly the paths the step changed inside its scope plus what the regeneration derives from them; a path outside the scope is never committed. After the caller's pre-advance step it refuses by name, installing nothing, an in-scope path edited in the checkout or a trunk that moved; it then installs the commit's paths and advances trunk by compare-and-swap, a refusal inside the install restoring what it wrote while leaving and naming any path edited there; and it removes the scratch worktree on success, refusal and crash, naming one that will not remove.

### LLR LLR-207
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: tree_identity folds raw NUL-delimited `git ls-tree -r -z` entries after dropping docs/reviews/, docs/log.d/ and docs/iteration/, and returns a 64-hex SHA-256 identity. fold_listing accepts bytes and preserves every path byte so display quoting or decoding cannot merge distinct work trees. A passed-checks record is exactly one column-0 label line, `Checks-Passed: tree=<40 hex> work=<40 hex> checks=<commit|landing|none> tests=<64 hex|none> <summary>`; its verifier returns fields only when tree and work equal the commit's own tree and first parent and its `checks` field is in that set. tests carries none for an empty named test-path set; otherwise it carries the SHA-256 of that set after path separators and leading ./ are normalized, duplicates are removed, and the paths are joined in sorted order. Thus one set in any order or spelling has one value, and a different set has a different value. refresh_passed_checks admits a refresh subject only with that verified record and checks `landing` or `none`, so work_tip never peels a commit record as a refresh; the read-only identity walk still steps through any commit whose non-record identity equals its parent, including an empty commit record. One producer writes every passed-checks record: it fixes the staged tree before the checks, refuses failing checks or one that leaves the index or worktree off that tree, and restores the index on every failed write; its line joins the message's final trailer block. The record-checks route refuses before the checks when commit hooks are off, the primary checkout holds the branch, the worktree is dirty (including untracked files), the commit-check declaration is absent or empty, the message carries the label, or the harness floor refuses; it reads the commit checks and derived test set from the lane's own declarations, with no default, and refuses before the checks when derivation refuses; a refusal leaves the branch and index unchanged. On the record-checks route the producer runs the commit checks then the derived files, records their digest in the unchanged grammar, and prints the set, listed evidence and reported gaps. A trailer without a `checks` field is not read. work_tip peels only a verified refresh at the tip for the reset path; governing_rev is read-only and walks across commits whose non-record identity equals their first parent's, peeling any DISPOSABLE commit it meets to reach one those commits would otherwise hide, and ending at the first commit whose identity differs from its parent's or at its absent-parent and depth bounds - so a branch with no disposable commit under it still walks. TWO commit classes are disposable and the read-only walk peels BOTH: the station's REFRESH, admitted by refresh_passed_checks, and the machinery's OWN adjudication close, admitted by mechanical_close_peel against exactly one parent and a no-renames changed-path stream lying wholly under docs/work/. That stream must pair deleted specs from exactly ONE docs/work/active/<branch>/ with additions of the same filenames under docs/work/complete/, and ONLY the move itself may create or destroy: an addition or deletion under docs/work/ that is not one half of a recognised move refuses the whole commit, since peeling it would measure a spec's injection or an archived row's destruction at the parent, where no reviewer saw it. Modifications ride along unrestricted, because a close's inbound relink rewrites other rows in place and only ever modifies. A mechanical close admits exactly one moved row; any other shape declines to peel toward more review. Its subject must equal the writer's composed subject exactly, so a public prefix/suffix with a forged middle is not a passed-checks record. The one-row clause refuses an empty close, which has no deletion, and every multi-row close; one deletion implies one source branch; and the subject composer takes a single id and refuses anything else, so no empty-id subject is ever composed. One property admits the two classes and it is not two rules: both are machine-authored end to end, and both move the tree without the lane having changed what it CLAIMS - docs/work/ is deliberately inside the identity because a spec's own claims are what a verdict is about, and the close carries those claims through unchanged. Every arm of both verifications fails toward REVIEW, since declining to peel measures at a later rev and can never ask for LESS - though not always for more: on the empty close the refused commit is identity-preserving, so the walk-through step reaches the same rev the peel would have, and the refusal guards this function's contract rather than any answer the gate gives. The two peels are NOT the same peel: work_tip calls refresh_passed_checks directly rather than through the shared disposable test, so a mechanical close is never removed by the destructive reset path, which may only ever drop a refresh sitting literally on the tip. governing_identity composes governing_rev and tree_identity for the branch tip or an explicit revision, including a round's reviewed commit and a trailer's carrier; the branch argument is a branch NAME, because the peel verifies a refresh commit against the branch it names and no refresh subject names HEAD. format_scope records a REVIEW session scope as full, narrow or final base..sha; gating_span admits a fresh full scope in either mode, or a final scope only where reviewer retention is on and final_reviewer is persisted; a narrow scope and a retained full scope never admit. logged_rounds joins branch-scoped round files to coordinator session logs by train and ordinal, and each round's review phase is the JOINED LOG's declared phase rather than the one its own filename carries, so a file cannot claim a phase its session did not serve; a joined key whose logs declare more than one review phase yields no round. It retains only a gating span whose base equals the caller's claim base and whose reviewed sha the round file names. round_entries retains only rounds whose reviewed revision governs the identity under judgement and emits phase, ordinal and verdict entries through an injected parser. filing_refusal applies one filing check: the first physical line is exactly the Reviewed full-sha opener (a CR ending is allowed; no leading blank or whitespace is), exactly one canonical VERDICT line, a finding count matching the file, and a change request naming a finding. An unreadable or refused round is an entry with no verdict, so it is the phase's latest word rather than an older verdict being used. tree_already_judged is true only for an entry carrying a verdict and false with no base. declared_phases clamps the configured independent-review count to the available review phases. phases_owed returns declared phases whose latest entry is missing or invalid evidence; a dissent is served. round_count returns the largest per-phase entry count at one governing identity, so a multi-phase review cycle counts once and changed work starts a new count. format_trailer and parse_trailer carry `Review-Verdict: APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED rounds=<N> tree=<64 hex>`. branch_trailers verifies each carrier against its governing identity and returns the ordered trailer sequence per tree. Readers treat unavailable repository answers according to their own fail-closed contract. The record-checks route reads the declared expected-failure mark with the commit checks from one parse of the lane's declarations, with no default, and refuses before either checks when either required declaration is unavailable or its mark transition is refused under LLR-368.
  - after: The tree identity folds the raw NUL-delimited recursive tree listing after dropping the review-record, log-fragment and iteration-record directories, and is a 64-hex SHA-256 identity. Its fold takes the listing as bytes and preserves every path byte, so display quoting or decoding cannot merge distinct work trees. The tests field carries none for an empty named test-path set; otherwise it carries the SHA-256 of that set after path separators and a leading current-directory prefix are normalized, duplicates are removed, and the paths are joined in sorted order. Thus one set in any order or spelling has one value, and a different set has a different value. The refresh check admits a refresh subject only with that verified record and a checks field of landing or none, so the reset path's tip peel never peels a commit record as a refresh; the read-only identity walk still steps through any commit whose non-record identity equals its parent, including an empty commit record. One producer writes every passed-checks record: it fixes the staged tree before the checks, refuses failing checks or one that leaves the index or worktree off that tree, and restores the index on every failed write; its line joins the message's final trailer block. The record-checks route refuses before the checks when commit hooks are off, the primary checkout holds the branch, the worktree is dirty (including untracked files), the commit-check declaration is absent or empty, the message carries the label, or the harness floor refuses; it reads the commit checks and derived test set from the lane's own declarations, with no default, and refuses before the checks when derivation refuses; a refusal leaves the branch and index unchanged. On the record-checks route the producer runs the commit checks then the derived files, records their digest in the unchanged grammar, and prints the set, listed evidence and reported gaps. A trailer without a checks field is not read. The reset path's tip peel removes only a verified refresh at the tip; the read-only identity walk walks across commits whose non-record identity equals their first parent's, peeling any disposable commit it meets to reach one those commits would otherwise hide, and ending at the first commit whose identity differs from its parent's or at its absent-parent and depth bounds - so a branch with no disposable commit under it still walks. Two commit classes are disposable and the read-only walk peels both: the station's refresh, admitted by the refresh check, and the machinery's own adjudication close, admitted by the mechanical-close check against exactly one parent and a no-renames changed-path stream lying wholly under the work registry. That stream must pair deleted specs from exactly one branch's active directory with additions of the same filenames under the completed directory, and only the move itself may create or destroy: an addition or deletion under the work registry that is not one half of a recognised move refuses the whole commit, since peeling it would measure a spec's injection or an archived row's destruction at the parent, where no reviewer saw it. Modifications ride along unrestricted, because a close's inbound relink rewrites other rows in place and only ever modifies. A mechanical close admits exactly one moved row; any other shape declines to peel toward more review. Its subject must equal the writer's composed subject exactly, so a public prefix and suffix with a forged middle is not a passed-checks record. The one-row clause refuses an empty close, which has no deletion, and every multi-row close; one deletion implies one source branch; and the subject composer takes a single id and refuses anything else, so no empty-id subject is ever composed. One property admits the two classes and it is not two rules: both are machine-authored end to end, and both move the tree without the lane having changed what it claims - the work registry is deliberately inside the identity because a spec's own claims are what a verdict is about, and the close carries those claims through unchanged. Every arm of both verifications fails toward review, since declining to peel measures at a later revision and can never ask for less - though not always for more: on the empty close the refused commit is identity-preserving, so the walk-through step reaches the same revision the peel would have, and the refusal guards the mechanical-close check's contract rather than any answer the gate gives. The two peels are not the same peel: the reset path's tip peel asks the refresh check directly rather than through the shared disposable test, so a mechanical close is never removed by the destructive reset path, which may only ever drop a refresh sitting literally on the tip. The governing identity composes the read-only walk and the tree identity for the branch tip or an explicit revision, including a round's reviewed commit and a trailer's carrier; the branch is given by its name, because the peel verifies a refresh commit against the branch it names and no refresh subject names HEAD. A review session's scope is recorded as full, narrow or final base..sha; the gating span admits a fresh full scope in either mode, or a final scope only where reviewer retention is on and the final reviewer is persisted; a narrow scope and a retained full scope never admit. The logged rounds join branch-scoped round files to coordinator session logs by train and ordinal, and each round's review phase is the joined log's declared phase rather than the one its own filename carries, so a file cannot claim a phase its session did not serve; a joined key whose logs declare more than one review phase yields no round. They retain only a gating span whose base equals the caller's claim base and whose reviewed commit the round file names. The round entries retain only rounds whose reviewed revision governs the identity under judgement and emit phase, ordinal and verdict entries through a verdict parser the caller supplies. One filing check applies: the first physical line is exactly the Reviewed full-sha opener (a CR ending is allowed; no leading blank or whitespace is), exactly one canonical VERDICT line, a finding count matching the file, and a change request naming a finding. A tree reads as already judged only for an entry carrying a verdict, and never with no base. The declared phases clamp the configured independent-review count to the available review phases. ### LLR LLR-262
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The coordinator's session log records each session's phase and its exact commit range (`# commits: before..after`), and records a REVIEW session's full or narrow scope (`# review-scope: base..sha`); session_range and parse_range read the range, review_logs yields the gating span with it, and range_paths lists the paths the range changed with renames split. scope_offenders names every changed path other than the REVIEW session's own verdict file, the round file named for the session's train, ordinal and logged phase under any reviewed sha and tag; record paths are not exempt. Right after a REVIEW session the loop asks scope_offenders of the range it just took and, on any offender, stops the run needing an operator with the paths named; a session log is append-only evidence, so every reader takes each log as the commit that ADDED it recorded it (log_history, review_logs), never at the branch tip, and the merge ladder's review-scope rung first refuses by name any commit in the lane that modified or deleted a session log, then asks the same function of every REVIEW session log the branch committed (review_scope_refusal) and refuses the merge by name, an unreadable log or range refusing too. After a REVIEW or CRITIQUE session, uncommitted changes not present before it, other than the coordinator's own session log, fail the draw through the existing failed-draw path - the route cooled and the same phase re-drawn - with those paths stashed under one entry named for the session, so the re-drawn session starts clean; when that stash fails the run stops needing an operator with the paths and the failure named, and nothing is re-drawn. read_verdict reads the verdict blob as committed at HEAD in both arms, so an uncommitted verdict is never routed on; session_body binds an adjudication's composed request beside its verdict before the loop calls it; after the call, run_iteration records the loop call's success from call_succeeded in plan, and adjudication_bookkeeping calls record_outcome against those bound kinds and uses its returned why as the completion obligation: a failed call is recorded failed, keeps that obligation and does not read its verdict. logged_rounds reads each round at the end of its session's recorded range, and only when that range changed the round file: a later commit rewriting the file changes nothing the gate counts, a file its session never committed is no round, and a key whose logs record different phases or ranges yields none. done_when.items reads a spec's Done-when section, through kitlib.registry.done_when_section, as items: a list item with its continuation lines, or a prose paragraph, across subsections. changes compares a claim-time text with a later one after stripping ticks, strikethrough and whitespace; a later item carries a claim-time one when it equals it or extends it with evidence in the form closed specs use - appended text opening with an evidence separator (a dash, an arrow, a bracket, a bar or a check mark) and carrying an evidence token (a capitalised completion word from the closed set LANDED, DONE, MET, VERIFIED, SHIPPED, PASSED, FIXED and COVERED, a path or test id, a backticked name, or a commit sha) - while any other appended text, after closing punctuation or not, is a change; and every claim-time item not carried is reported as changed and every later item carrying none as added. The claim warns by name on stderr for each claimed row with no Done-when item and still claims. When a review round is composed, the brief gains a block quoting each change between the spec under active/<train>/ at the lane's base and the lane's own copy. At merge, intake evaluates each claimed non-adjudication row. An unreadable claim refuses the merge-time mint, naming why; the merge itself stands and recovery is a trunk-side fix. For an absent claim, intake emits its stderr line that the Done-when check did not run and mints nothing. For a readable claim whose closed Done-when changed against trunk's pre-merge copy under active/<branch>, intake proceeds through the existing arms: when no verdict line or operator ruling in the merged tree binds the closed text's digest, it mints one brief-bearing done-when adjudication row, titled per row and branch so a re-run dedupes, with Adjudicates naming the closed work item and Context quoting each change; when a covering SUCCESSOR verdict carries Dispositions drafts, it mints the Done-when part's own drafts instead - the whole single-kind verdict or its one ## done-when section - and refuses repeated Dispositions inside that valid section or one supplying no draft. A sitting with no or repeated done-when section, or a DONE-WHEN line under another heading, is invalid, covers nothing, and leaves the uncovered arm to mint the goalposts row. review_logs reads the resolved final-reviewer mode once per call through reviewer_dials and passes it to every REVIEW log's gating_span, so all readers apply one mode.

## SR-157 — Spine and work-registry rules fail the harness verdict

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (the tracked work-item graph the loop derives its next work from is kept coherent). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.

- **LLR-ID**: LLR-421

- **Title**: An interface whose reach through the rows constraining it and the cases naming it holds no executable test case is reported
- **Detail**: An interface's reach is the design rows and system requirements whose constrains cell names it, the design rows and requirements a test case naming it verifies, and every test case naming it or verifying one of those rows, a constraining design row's cases including those of the design rows below it in the requirement tree, as a constraining requirement's include those of the design rows decomposing it. Each live interface whose reach holds no test case the harness runs, an observation case being one it does not run, is named on one warning line, and those whose reach holds observation cases alone are named on a line of their own. A count line gives the interfaces reached through a constraining design row, through a constraining requirement, through a naming test case alone, by observation alone, and not at all. The process settings' interface-check opt-out silences the report, and with no live interface row it is empty. The report does not change the strict check's exit code.
- **Rationale**: Every interface is pointed at by a requirement stating its behaviour or by a test case observing it, and reaches a test the harness runs that way; reporting the reach before it fails lets the population land first.

- **Status**: Drafted

## SR-158 — Documentation drift fails or warns per its declared tier

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a pass never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.


### LLR LLR-012
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Resolves intra-repo links, requires a PROJECT-VISION tag, and checks generated-doc freshness under --stale. A dead intra-repo link or an absent PROJECT-VISION tag fails; a clean doc set passes. Wired as check.py's built-in doc-navigability step, running from DevStg-Reqs on; orphans warn unless --strict-orphans, with docs/orphans-allow as the reviewed carve-out, and --stale is warn-only.
  - after: Resolves intra-repository links, requires the project-vision tag, and in its staleness mode checks generated-document freshness. A dead intra-repository link or an absent project-vision tag fails, and a clean document set passes. The gate harness runs it as its built-in documentation step from DevStg-Reqs on; an orphaned document warns unless orphans are made strict, with the declared orphan allowlist as the reviewed carve-out, and the staleness mode only warns.

A symbol reference, naming a module and a public name, is resolved against the public-symbol inventory scanned from the source's syntax tree, a module's tail name mapping to its public symbols. Each dangling finding is printed naming the token at fault, and the untraced count is printed on every run. The declared-absences file is the reviewed absence carve-out: a reference to a path it lists, one path and its reason per line, is reported untraced with that reason quoted rather than dangling, and a line carrying no reason separator declares nothing.

  - after: Path and symbol reference checker

## SR-167 — Performance-budget breaches fail their hard-gated rows

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall fail the checks it runs exactly when an in-tier hard-gated performance-budget row breaches — its measured metric worse than its absolute Budget, or regressed beyond its declared Tolerance band around the committed baseline — while warn-gated rows and out-of-tier rows only warn, and a metric with no measurement this run is a reported skip, never a silent pass.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a pass — a breach on a row the project chose to hard-gate must fail the run, not ride as a tracked number) and SN-012 (the perf layer is opt-in; a repo declaring no PB rows pays nothing, while one that does gets the same honest-gate discipline as every other check). This row is the requirement home for the perf verdict itself: SR-006 states the step plan and the missing-tool refusal, SR-015 the PB back-link invariant, and SR-033 only the warn-tier, never-fails side, so the exit contract the perf step already implements, decomposes and proves had no requirement stating it. One row for BOTH breach arms deliberately — absolute and regression are one delivered exit contract, the SR-157 one-verdict shape, and a regression-only framing is the symptom, not the boundary. THE REQUIREMENT NAMES NO ARTIFACT: the comparator is not on the declared delivered-artifact list — it is the harness's internal perf step — so the SR states the harness-verdict observable and the comparator is named only in acceptance as the current carrier, the SR-157/SR-158 pattern. Vacuous in THIS repository — no performance-budgets registry exists here and the perf layer is declared absent — but the template ships the layer, and a shipped layer owes a stated, tested obligation even where locally unused. One act is deliberately still owed: LLR-014.sr_refs and TC-014.verifies are NOT re-pointed here — both rows are Accepted, so amending their cells overrides acceptance, which i… [455 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-014
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Compares measured metrics (docs/test/perf-metrics.json) to declared budgets and the committed baseline (docs/test/perf-baseline.json) and fails beyond tolerance at the row's block/warn level; --update-baseline is the reviewed acceptance path for an intended regression. The step is wired into the harness at DevStg-Impl, per its declared gates in docs/stack.ini.
  - after: Compares the measured metrics to the declared budgets and the committed baseline and fails beyond tolerance at the row's block or warn level; the baseline update is the reviewed acceptance path for an intended regression. Each in-tier budget that fails or warns prints a line naming its status and its budget id; a fail-level budget with no measurement this run is reported on its own line as a skip rather than passing in silence, while a warn-level budget with no measurement prints no line of its own and is counted among the skips. The run prints an OK or FAIL summary counting the failures, warnings and skips, a project with no budget rows printing an OK line saying so and budgets with no metrics file a SKIP line. A comparison exits 1 only when an in-tier budget fails, and 0 for every other outcome, warnings and skips included. A run that reaches a comparison writes the performance report, naming each failing budget's reason. The step is wired into the harness at DevStg-Impl, per its declared gates in the stack profile.

  - after: IF-004;IF-147;IF-167

## SR-168 — The state view shows current progress and next work

> **Requirement.** The delivered generator set shall produce one state view from which a reviewer reads the adopted repository's completeness, its requirement decomposition, its planned and in-flight work, and any declared hold on that work, without consulting a second surface.

> **Rationale.** Realizes SN-023's FIRST conjunct — a reviewer sees the project's progress from one dashboard-like file — with SN-010 (what the reviewer reads there is the generated truth, never a hand-kept summary of it). ONE DECISION PER ROW: the integrity of a generated artifact (SR-070) and what that artifact must SHOW are two obligations that fail independently. This row is the progress half; SR-169 is the connection half, along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws. Deliberately NOT folded back into SR-070: a state view that regenerates deterministically, offline and drift-checkably while omitting the work in flight satisfies that row completely and this one not at all. Deliberately NOT folded into SR-169 either: a complete progress view can carry no seam graph whatsoever — the state this repository was in before its interface registry existed — so the two are separately observable and separately buildable. `phase` inherits the parent's 1 rather than tracking its children (which span 1 through 4), because a phase records WHEN a piece shipped, and child-later-than-parent is the ordinary case.

### LLR LLR-198
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The pending-operator-action derivation, in one module that renders no page and decides nothing about lanes. Its two committed-tree-pure, clock-free sources are spine_pending, which names each Drafted SR needing a first acceptance and each SR whose text has drifted from its copy under docs/archive/last_accepted needing a re-accept, one pointer line each naming the on-demand brief that carries the depth; and pause_pending, which names the tracked docs/work/pause and fails closed on a file it cannot read. pending_items joins the two into the TYPED model - one PendingItem per action carrying its kind and its rendered bullet. pending_block is that model rendered, the byte-compared content of the generated operator surface's pending region, and operator_cards is the model minus the pause, which is what a drained-queue banner counts. THE SITING IS THE ROW: the dashboard status snapshot, the generated operator surface and the dispatcher's exit banner all read this derivation, and only the first is inside the render family, so defining it there forced the other two through the gen_trajectory facade and into a private name. traj_status.py re-exports the three surviving names (spine_pending, pause_pending, pending_block) under their former names, so no caller moved to gain the layer and the generated surfaces are byte-identical - which is why that split sibling belongs to this row beside the read model: the shim is part of what this row delivers, not a leftover.
  - after: The pending-operator-action derivation sits in one module that renders no page and decides nothing about lanes. Its two committed-tree-pure, clock-free sources are the spine source, which names each Drafted SR needing a first acceptance and each SR whose text has drifted from its recorded copy and needs a re-accept, one pointer line each naming the on-demand brief that carries the depth, and the pause source, which names the tracked pause file and fails closed on a file it cannot read. The two join into one typed model, one item per action carrying its kind and its rendered line. The rendered block, the byte-compared content of the generated operator surface's pending region, is that model rendered, and the operator cards are the model less the pause, which is what a drained-queue banner counts. The dashboard status snapshot, the generated operator surface and the dispatcher's exit banner all read this derivation, and the dashboard status module still answers for the spine source, the pause source and the rendered block under their former names, so no caller moved and the generated surfaces stay byte-identical.
- **Rationale**
  - before: A derivation every layer reads must sit below all of them, and this one did not: it lived inside a render module of the traj_* family, reachable only through a re-export facade of about a thousand lines. The repository review recorded the consequence twice - a large facade imported for a state query, and private names used as a cross-module API - and the second was DOCUMENTED as an interface row rather than removed, which is the failure mode this kit's own doctrine names: a seam registry records crossings, it does not license them. Extracting the derivation cut both edges at their cause and left the facade with no importers at all, which is now asserted rather than described. The typed item is what makes the banner's exclusion of the pause a declared filter over a kind field instead of a second opinion about what is pending: the ruled amendment requires that the banner and the operator surface can never disagree, and one read model with one filter is that requirement held by construction rather than by three callers agreeing to be careful. A generic shared module was refused for the same reason it is refused everywhere here, and the package of stdlib-only shared helpers by a hard rule rather than by taste - every module there must stay import-clean of the rest of the scripts directory so the scaffolder can import it, while this module reads the work registry through the validator's loaders and the spine through its sibling parser.
  - after: A derivation every layer reads must sit below all of them, and this one did not: it lived inside a render module of the dashboard family, reachable only through a re-export facade of about a thousand lines. The repository review recorded the consequence twice, a large facade imported for a state query and private names used as a cross-module interface, and the second was documented as an interface row rather than removed: a seam registry records crossings, it does not license them. Extracting the derivation cut both edges at their cause and left the facade with no importers at all, which is now asserted rather than described. The typed item makes the banner's exclusion of the pause a declared filter over a kind field instead of a second opinion about what is pending, so the banner and the operator surface cannot disagree, by one read model with one filter rather than by three callers agreeing to be careful. A generic shared module was refused, and the package of stdlib-only shared helpers by a hard rule rather than by taste: every module there must stay import-clean of the rest of the scripts directory so the scaffolder can import it, while this module reads the work registry through the validator's loaders and the spine through its sibling parser.

## SR-170 — Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.

> **Rationale.** ONE DECISION PER ROW: a shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. This row holds the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract alone; ordering-and-all-or-nothing is SR-173's and identity allocation SR-174's, each with its own children. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). The serial LANDING (SR-156) and the rule that shared authority surfaces regenerate only there are two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already ruled for the identity mint — a work branch never mints a work-item id — and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.

Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op.
  - after: Compiles the session log fragments into the log, appending each through the log's shared appender, in the order of the commits that added them, oldest first, with the file name breaking a tie. Relative links are rebased from the fragment directory to the log's, fragments are deleted after the append, and the compile is all-or-nothing on any validation error, a refused compile leaving the log unchanged and every fragment in place. Dotfiles and the directory's own readme are not fragments and are never removed. Never commits; the caller owns the commit. The artifact-regen half is LLR-142. A fragment-free run is a clean idempotent no-op. Asked for the compile, the regeneration, or neither, which runs both with the compile first since it can move the log the generators read, the step runs exactly those; a dry run prints what each selected operation would do and writes nothing. The step exits 0 when every selected operation finished or found nothing to do, and 1 on any failure, naming the offending fragment or generator on its error stream; a failed compile stops the step before the regeneration.

## SR-178 — Text that has moved away from its acceptance record is reported

> **Requirement.** The kit shall report any recorded artifact whose text has moved away from the
copy recording its acceptance - stakeholder needs included - regardless of any
Status movement.

> **Rationale.** THE DRIFT half, separable from SR-140 in the direction that matters: a record that exists and rides its acceptance commit, with nothing ever comparing live text against it, satisfies SR-140 completely and leaves every later amendment invisible. That is not a hypothetical - it is precisely the failure the git-history derivation had, and the reason a baseline on disk was owed at all.
REGARDLESS OF STATUS MOVEMENT is the load-bearing clause, not a qualifier. The amendment machinery watches the Status flip, and the sanctioned staged-amendment path deliberately does not flip - so a rule keyed off the flip returns nothing on exactly the rows a sitting exists to judge. Reporting against the recorded copy is the only reading that survives that, because the copy is a baseline OUTSIDE the live file and a walk over the live file can never be one.
THE STAKEHOLDER-NEED RIDER is in the normative text rather than left to the acceptance because the need tier is the one a sitting most wants to hold and the one the amendment-watching rules were last to reach: each rule walks the tiers it is given, so without the clause the need tier is left off the walk and no rule reaches it. Needs are compared row by row under the same accepted and traced split as every other tier.
WHAT COUNTS AS MOVEMENT is one definition with one home, shared with the amend-without-flip guard: the id column and the Status cell are excluded STRUCTURALLY (the id is the join key rather than content, and folding Status in woul… [817 more chars — read the registry row]

## SR-205 — With the assumption gate on, the boundary rung requires accepted, active assumptions that reach their stakeholders

> **Requirement.** Where the assumption gate is enabled, the delivered harness shall fail the boundary gate for each system requirement not recorded as coincident and not delivered jointly with a sibling that shares one of its needs, that cites no assumption, and for each requirement that cites an assumption which is not accepted, not active, does not reach the stakeholders of its needs, or is a fidelity assumption naming a surrogate that is not accepted.

> **Rationale.** Without a gate the assumption rows are optional documentation: a requirement could cite a draft, a falsified premise or one landing nowhere its stakeholders are, and still pass. At the boundary rung only maturity can be judged, because test results do not exist until the harness has run; evidence is the release rung's question. The gate is opt-in because it asks for a written argument behind every requirement, a cost a small project may rightly decline.


### LLR LLR-242
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: boundary_gate_findings(srs, das, surs, reach, have_frame) returns nothing when have_frame is false. With a frame, it fails each requirement with no Coincident whose DA-Refs is empty, unless the requirement is joint (its Delivered-With names a declared sibling sharing one of its SN-Refs, the class `_classify` already gives it), or that cites an assumption which is not Accepted, whose standing is not active, which the reach check reports, or whose realized_by surrogate is not Accepted; each finding names the requirement, the assumption and the unmet condition. A frame with no assumptions registry therefore fails every requirement not recorded coincident, since enabling the gate asks for the argument. assumption_gate_enabled(docs) reads [checks] assumption_gate from the policy file, where an absent or unreadable key reads off. check.py gains a built-in step `assumption-gate` at the DevStg-Boundary threshold, named in BUILTIN_STEP_NAMES; with the gate off the same findings print as advisories and the step passes. The findings never enter the stage derivation. With the gate off, check_assumption_gate's `_fold_reach_caused` passes only the reach-caused advisories (those absent when the same judgement runs with an empty reach map) through held_until_agreed; every other advisory stays listed, and a failure is never folded.
  - after: The boundary half of the assumption gate reports nothing when the project declares no frame. With a frame, it fails each requirement with no coincident cell whose assumption references are empty, unless the requirement is joint (its delivered-with cell names a declared sibling sharing one of its need references, the class the requirement classifier already gives it), or that cites an assumption which is not Accepted, whose standing is not active, which the reach check reports, or whose realizing surrogate is not Accepted; each finding names the requirement, the assumption and the unmet condition. A frame with no assumptions registry therefore fails every requirement not recorded coincident, since enabling the gate asks for the argument. The gate is on when the policy file's checks table sets its assumption-gate dial, an absent or unreadable key reading off. The harness gains a built-in assumption-gate step at the DevStg-Boundary threshold, named among its built-in steps; with the gate on, each finding prints as a failure line and the step exits 1, and with it off the same findings print as advisories and the step passes, each run ending on a summary line naming the step. The findings never enter the stage derivation. With the gate off, only the reach-caused advisories (those absent when the same judgement runs with an empty reach) are folded into one advisory line counting them while no stakeholder is agreed; every other advisory stays listed, and a failure is never folded.

## SR-208 — Held-rung changes are refused before they land

> **Requirement.** Where the declared acceptance level holds a snapshotted registry's rung for an operator, the delivered harness shall refuse, before it lands on trunk, both a lane's acceptance claim that is not a CLARITY re-acceptance bound to the row's judged text and a marked autonomous commit outside a lane that moves a held status or makes a held acceptance claim.

> **Rationale.** The acceptance level names the rungs an operator still accepts, and an automated acceptance of the spine's own rows is already refused. The frame, assumption, surrogate and component registries carry status cells of their own that no automated path was stopped from changing, because the one setting that would stop it defaults to allowing. A policy that declares a rung operator-held without refusing the automated path is a claim, not a control. A lane can carry an acceptance into trunk even when no automated writer made its commit, and an autonomous commit outside a lane can make the same change; protecting only selected writers leaves the held state dependent on who delivered it. Binding the permitted re-acceptance to its judged text prevents a verdict about one text authorizing another, while leaving an unmarked reviewed trunk acceptance to its operator authority.

## SR-209 — Every commit the loop makes carries a checked provenance trailer

> **Requirement.** When a commit is made from a process the unattended loop started, or is merged in a lane the loop owns from the point the loop took that lane over, the delivered commit floor shall refuse it unless it carries a valid provenance trailer naming the loop and its session.

> **Rationale.** A status change on an operator-held rung can be told apart from an operator's only if every loop-made commit says it is one. Without a floor that holds every loop commit to it, a trailer could appear on only some of the loop's commits and a missing one would prove nothing. Where a commit is made, the condition is who made it, not when: a person committing in another checkout while the loop runs is not the loop. Where a lane merges, the floor sees commits, not processes, so it reads whose lane it is from the commits and judges every commit of a loop lane, a person's included: there an unmarked commit the loop made cannot be told from a person's, and a person's commit belongs in the person's own lane. The commits made before the loop took a lane over are exempt, since nothing then could have marked them. Refusing the unmarked commit at the floor makes the mark total, which is what lets a later check treat its absence as meaningful. The floor where a commit is made is local and a determined bypass defeats it, which is why the merge re-checks a loop lane and the history check stands behind both.

The dispatcher's run and the loop's main entry, outside interactive mode, set the marker in their own process environment once per run, never in a launcher script, so child sessions inherit it and a person's shell does not.

## SR-215 — At a checkpoint, an unjudged, expired or triggered observation test is queued once for re-judging

> **Requirement.** When a work item merges, a release is prepared or a stage gate is checked, the delivered harness shall file one re-judge work item per due observation case: one with no result, one whose result expired, or one whose declared trigger fires after its work-item floor; with no declared trigger, a change to its declared inputs makes it due, subject to that floor. It keeps at most one open item per case, and reports each observation case that cites no written pass criteria as a warning.

> **Rationale.** Judgements cost time and model calls and vary across sessions. Written pass criteria fixed before the first judgement make the pass reviewable. The closed-work floor and the declared trigger are cost limits the operator directed (the PERFORMANCE lens): no change or checkpoint makes an accepted judgement due again within the configured number of closed work items of its latest record, and a declared trigger replaces input changes with the change or checkpoint it names, so a judgement can stand on changes it never judged until a qualifying change or checkpoint meets the floor or its result expires. First judgement and expiry keep missing or old evidence from standing indefinitely.


### LLR LLR-254
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A sibling of consolidate.py imported by intake. observation_test_cases(root, rev) reads observation cases at a revision. checkpoint_for(root, rev, tc) returns the case's Trigger when it names the release or stage-gate checkpoint, else merge. due_cases(root, rev, now, checkpoint) reads committed inputs and records and uses the cadence decision to apply the configured closed-WI floor and declared trigger. A case with no record is due at once, and an expired record is due at once; neither waits for the floor. Without a trigger, a changed input digest is due subject to the floor; without inputs, only absence and expiry make a case due. checkpoint_drafts drafts each due case once, suppressing an open item by its typed Brief and Adjudicates cells, never by title. A draft names the case, the reason, the rubric and the changed inputs or the fired trigger, and its title carries the case id and the inputs-digest prefix. Committed links are excluded by the observation writer's own link predicate. No model runs.
  - after: The checkpoint re-judge decision sits beside the consolidation census, and the intake imports it. It reads the observation test cases at a given revision. A case answers to the checkpoint its trigger cell names when that is the release or stage-gate checkpoint, and to the merge checkpoint otherwise. Which cases are due is read from the committed inputs and records at the revision, the cadence decision applying the configured closed-work-item floor and the declared trigger. A case with no record is due at once, and an expired record is due at once; neither waits for the floor. Without a trigger, a changed input digest is due subject to the floor; without inputs, only absence and expiry make a case due. The checkpoint's drafts draft each due case once, suppressing an open item by its typed Brief and Adjudicates cells, never by title. A draft names the case, the reason, the rubric and the changed inputs or the fired trigger, and its title carries the case id and the inputs-digest prefix. Committed links are excluded by the observation writer's own link predicate. No model runs.

### LLR LLR-265
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: `intake.py sweep --before <sha> --after <sha> --branch <lane> --merged WI-###[;WI-###]` runs intake_after_merge over the range with an outcomes map holding exactly the named rows: merged_outcomes reads each one's outcome from the one terminal folder holding its spec (complete, partial or cancelled), so the early-close dispositions, the clean-close spot checks and the drafts a merged adjudication row names in its Dispositions section are minted for those rows and no others, beside the range's diff triggers (the amendment and first-acceptance rows) and the merge checkpoint's re-judge drafts at `--after`. `--merged` judges one merge, so _merged_shape_refusal refuses it without both range ends, when the two name one commit, and without `--branch`. The branch is the claim identity the Done-when check reads: a named row whose claim is under `active/<branch>/` at `--before` is checked against it, and one whose claim is not there gets one stderr line naming the row, the branch and why the check did not run; `--branch` names the mint commit either way, and a range or history sweep hands the check no claim identity. A named row in no terminal folder, or in more than one, refuses the whole sweep by name and mints nothing. `--merged` and `--with-terminal` are mutually exclusive; the range shape without either still judges no close, and the bare sweep still judges every close on file. Idempotent by the mint's exact-title dedup, like every sweep.
  - after: The intake's sweep command, given a range's two ends, the lane branch and the rows the merge closed, runs the merge slot's intake over the range with an outcomes map holding exactly the named rows: each one's outcome is read from the one terminal folder holding its spec (complete, partial or cancelled), so the early-close dispositions, the clean-close spot checks and the drafts a merged adjudication row names in its Dispositions section are minted for those rows and no others, beside the range's diff triggers (the amendment and first-acceptance rows) and the merge checkpoint's re-judge drafts at the range's later end. Naming merged rows judges one merge, so the sweep refuses it without both range ends, when the two name one commit, and without the branch. The branch is the claim identity the Done-when check reads: a named row whose claim is in the branch's active claim folder at the range's earlier end is checked against it, and one whose claim is not there gets one standard-error line naming the row, the branch and why the check did not run; the branch names the mint commit either way, and a range or history sweep hands the check no claim identity. A named row in no terminal folder, or in more than one, refuses the whole sweep by name and mints nothing. Naming merged rows and asking for the terminal scan are mutually exclusive; the range shape without either still judges no close, and the bare sweep still judges every close on file. Idempotent by the mint's exact-title dedup, like every sweep. A sweep that mints exits 0 naming how many rows it minted, as does one with nothing to mint, saying so; a refusal of a named-row sweep, for its range, its branch or a row's terminal folder, mints nothing and exits 1 with its reason on standard error.

## SR-216 — Each change is measured by the project's declared readability measures, and a worsening is reported with the part it worsens

> **Requirement.** When a change is submitted for acceptance, the delivered harness shall report together every worsening that the project's declared readability and structure measures find in the parts the change touches, naming each measure and part.

> **Rationale.** Code stays readable only if getting worse is visible when it happens; a report after the fact arrives once other work is already built on the worse code. Each measure keeps its own requirement and its own stamped baseline — cognitive complexity against a per-function baseline is one — and this row gathers what they find for the change in front of the reviewer, so no measure's comparison is restated here. A project with no declared measure has nothing to report, and that is itself reported, because the need's promise cannot be met by silence. Reporting rather than refusing keeps the process proportionate: a project opts a measure into gating when it wants that cost.

## SR-217 — A requirement implemented before its test cases were accepted is reported

> **Requirement.** The delivered harness shall report each system requirement accepted after the project's declared starting point whose implementation has landed on trunk while any of its test cases was not yet accepted for it, reading the order from the committed history.

> **Rationale.** Tests written after the code tend to describe what the code does rather than what was required, so the order is the evidence that a passing run means the requirement is met. The stage ladder puts test cases before implementation for a project as a whole, but not requirement by requirement, and the committed history is the one record of order that no cell can restate after the fact. Code declaring which requirement or design row it implements is the one event that marks an implementation landing. A requirement's acceptance is its entry into scope, so judging only requirements accepted after a declared starting point keeps a project that adopts the rule from being judged on history written before it; a project that declares none is judged whole. A test case is evidence for a requirement only once it is accepted while it names that requirement: dated from its own acceptance alone, a test case accepted for something else could be re-pointed at code already written and lend it an order it never had. A test case not yet accepted is the same gap at its widest, since its passing run shows only that the code does what unaccepted text says; the report names it with a warning rather than withholding the result, which a reviewer can still weigh.

## SR-218 — The state view shows, for each need, the assumptions it relies on and whether any has been shown false

> **Requirement.** The delivered state view shall show, for each stakeholder need, the assumptions its requirements rely on, with each assumption's status, validity and falsifier and the test cases whose failure shows it false.

> **Rationale.** The need promises that a reviewer can see what each outcome relies on and whether any of it has been shown false. The per-assumption reports and the acceptance brief answer other questions — what is wrong now, and what is being accepted — and neither lets a reviewer start from an outcome. Deriving the view from the registries keeps it from going stale the way a hand-written summary does, and marking a falsified premise in words as well as color keeps the signal readable for a reader who does not see color.

_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
