  - before: traj_parse.need_assumptions(root) derives, for each need, the assumptions cited by its requirements, each once, with its status, its standing, its falsifier, the test cases naming it in Assumption-Refs and the requirements citing it, and marks a need with an unclassified requirement and one whose requirements are all coincident. traj_views.need_assumption_block renders that list into the need's detail panel in the spine view; the test cases appear as the cases that can falsify it; a falsified assumption carries a text label as well as its color, and no evidence level is shown. With no assumptions registry the block is empty and the page is byte-identical to today's.
  - after: The per-need assumption derivation gives, for each need, the assumptions cited by its requirements, each once, with its status, its standing, its falsifier, the test cases naming it in Assumption-Refs and the requirements citing it, and marks a need with an unclassified requirement and one whose requirements are all coincident. The spine view renders that list into the need's detail panel: the test cases appear as the cases that can falsify it, a falsified assumption carries a text label as well as its colour, and no evidence level is shown. With no assumptions registry the block is empty and the generated page is byte-identical to the stored page without it.

## SR-220 — Overlapping queued work is consolidated through one judgement per queue state

> **Requirement.** While the queued work items include a set that overlaps, the delivered loop content shall hand that set to a single judgement, at most once for any one state of the queued work items and never while another judgement is in progress, queued over an item of the set, or queued to run before it, whose outcome takes effect only as a recorded restructuring in which each absorbed item is closed in a terminal state naming its one successor, its scope text unchanged.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that no human curate what comes next; its acceptance states only that the next work derives from the tracked work-item graph, which a stranger reading it would derive as work selection alone. This row states what that promise needs once the loop also mints work: rows minted one finding at a time overlap, and without this row either a human merges them, which is the curation the need rules out, or the loop builds the same job more than once. Two lenses shape it. UNATTENDED-OPS: an overlap is a standing state, so a judgement fired on it re-asks the same question on every idle tick unless the judged state is remembered past the judging row's close, an unbounded retry that pages nobody. PERFORMANCE: each judgement is a strong-tier session, so asking once per queue state is the operating-cost bound rather than an optimisation. The restructuring is recorded rather than done by deletion so that an absorbed item's scope and its successor stay readable after the act. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name the loop keeping its own queue free of duplicated work; until it does, this row is derived and says so. Choosing the next work stays SR-148's, and reporting an overlap as a warning stays SR-157's.


### LLR LLR-264
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: `intake.py consolidate` runs consolidate.census_draft over the checkout's registry. When the census proposes nothing it prints the census's reason on stdout and exits 0, because no overlap, an already-judged queue state and a judgement already over these rows are ordinary answers. Otherwise it prints the candidate set, its Digests pair and each pre-filter finding, then mints the one row through _mint, the one allocator of a work-item id, as one bookkeeping commit, and exits 0; a mint refusal prints on stderr and exits 1. `--dry-run` prints the same census and mints nothing.
  - after: The intake's consolidate command runs the consolidation census over the checkout's work registry. When the census proposes nothing it prints the census's reason on standard output and exits 0, because no overlap, an already-judged queue state and a judgement already over these rows are ordinary answers. Otherwise it prints the candidate set, its Digests pair and each pre-filter finding, then mints the one row through the mint, the one allocator of a work-item id, as one bookkeeping commit, and exits 0; a mint refusal prints on standard error and exits 1. Its dry run prints the same census and mints nothing.

## SR-222 — Every model session is recorded in one usage record, whichever provider CLI serves it

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall record every model session it launches, whichever provider command-line runner serves it, in one usage record: token usage under a published usage vocabulary pinned to a named revision, input counted inclusive of cached input, fresh input derived by one formula, the runner's raw usage kept verbatim, the route's provider filled from its roster row for every routed call, the runner named, the vocabulary's gen_ai.provider.name holding the runner's default provider for claude and codex and empty for any other runner, with runner reconfiguration to another provider not detected, and the context occupancy of the session's latest request kept apart from its billed tokens; for a runner other than claude, codex and opencode, the runner name is left empty, and so is every count or raw usage the loop cannot read from the runner's output.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that the operator can configure several model families and route work among them per job and capability level; it does not name a usage record, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for measurements that cannot be compared, or are unavailable, being read as a reliable total: routing among families is a cost and quality choice, and the choice is blind when one family's calls record their usage and another's do not, or when the runners disagree on what "input tokens" means (two count cached input apart from input, one counts it inside), so one column would hold two incompatible numbers. The consistency lens listens for readers disagreeing after a one-sided change: when each launching role keeps its own launch and logging path, a usage fix lands in one path and not the others. A published vocabulary avoids inventing a log format for a problem that is not novel; because it has no tagged release, the record pins the exact revision it follows. The raw usage is kept verbatim so a mapping later found wrong is re-derived rather than lost. Occupancy is kept apart from billed tokens because they answer different questions: billed tokens are summed over every request the session made, while how full a context is depends on the latest request alone, and a session reset that read the first as the second would fire on sessions that are nowhere near full. A runner who… [376 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

## SR-223 — A guarded session receives the guardrails payload vendored for its model

> **Requirement.** Where a repository vendors a guardrails payload for a model-name substring, the delivered loop content shall inject that payload, in place of the default guardrails core, into a guarded session whose model name contains that substring.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 asks that models be selected per job and capability level; it does not name what a session on a given model is told, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A guardrails posture written for one model is ritual noise to a stronger one and too little for a weaker one, and in an unattended run nobody watches a session drift: one core for every guarded model is the silent degrade that lens listens for. Selecting by a substring of the model name reuses the matcher the guardrails policy already applies, so one grammar decides both whether a session is guarded and what it is given, and the repository's own file names carry the mapping. A per-model key in the policy dial was the alternative, and it lost because a model name in shared configuration is the name that rots when models turn over. Fed back to the need: SN-026's acceptance could name a per-model posture; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-280
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: guardrails_core(root, model) lists the vendored core.<substring>.md payloads beside docs/guardrails/core.md, orders their substrings longest first and then by name, and reads the first one that guardrails_apply matches against the model name, reading core.md when none matches or no model is named. The KIT CORE block is extracted from the chosen file as from the core, and an unreadable chosen file returns None, which compose_session_prompt already reports once and runs past. Whether a session is guarded stays compose_session_prompt's call on the policy, made before the payload is read.
  - after: The guardrails payload reader lists the payloads a repository vendors beside its default guardrails core, each a core file named for a model-name substring, orders their substrings longest first and then by name, and reads the first one the guardrails policy's own matcher matches against the model name, reading the default core when none matches or no model is named. The block between the KIT CORE markers is extracted from the chosen file as from the default core, and an unreadable chosen file reads as no core, which the session prompt's composition reports once and runs past without one. Whether a session is guarded stays that composition's call on the policy, made before the payload is read.

## SR-224 — A skill whose description cannot say when to use it fails the skills-index check

> **Requirement.** The kit shall hold every skill description to the declared description floor: the descriptions it ships are at or above it, and its skills-index check fails, naming the skill, on any description shorter than it.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-005 asks that agents and humans work from the same playbook; it does not name what a skill's description must carry, so this obligation arrives through the first-run-adopter lens rather than through the need's text. An agent reads only a skill's description to decide whether to load it, so a description too short to say when to use the skill is shipped guidance an adopter's agent never reads: the skill works only for someone who already knows it exists, the undocumented-knowledge failure that lens listens for. A length floor is a lexical proxy, not a proof that a description is good: it catches the one-line stub that cannot state a trigger and leaves the wording to review. A reviewer's judgement of every description was the alternative, and it lost as the only guard because nothing would re-ask it when a skill is added. Fed back to the need: SN-005's acceptance could name the skills' descriptions as part of the playbook; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

_accepted — re-acceptance owed_

The regenerating path does not apply the floor, so a short description is refused where the index is checked rather than silently written.
  - after: The shortest skill description the skills index check accepts is one declared floor of 100 characters, room for a trigger and an effect ("Use when <trigger> - <what>"). The check applies the floor before it compares the index: it prints one line marked SHORT for each skill whose description is under the floor, naming the skill, its length and the floor, and then exits 1 without comparing the index; a description at the floor passes. Regenerating the index does not apply the floor, so a short description is refused where the index is checked rather than silently written.
- **Rationale**
  - before: The floor is one declared number read by one function, so the check and its tests agree on it; a length written into the message and the comparison separately would drift. Refusing at --check rather than at regeneration keeps the generator total: the index still renders, and the gate that reads the generator's exit code is the one that says the description needs rewriting.
  - after: The floor is one declared number, so the check and its tests agree on it; a length written into the message and the comparison separately would drift. Refusing at the check rather than at regeneration keeps the generator total: the index still renders, and the gate that reads the check's exit code is the one that says the description needs rewriting.

## SR-225 — A delegated run closes with a record of the calls it made, where the declared dial asks for one

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop content shall keep the operator's verdict on each delegated decision coupled to the work that acts on it and each delegated run to its record: whatever the declared decision-recording dial, reporting without refusing a record entry that omits a required disclosure field or leaves one blank, treating each run's record as the listing the operator reads, in which an entry with no verdict is not yet seen, and refusing an overrule unless its transition change files or amends open work that cites the entry; and, where that dial asks for a record, refusing to integrate a closing lane whose record is absent, naming where it belongs.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-029 asks that a run released to automation get as far as it honestly can, and that an acceptance it makes on a released tier leave a record naming who made it; it does not name the other calls a delegated run makes on the operator's behalf, the ones too settled to hold the run for and not settled enough to be history, so this obligation arrives through the unattended-operations lens rather than through the need's text. A call nobody is told about is the failure that lens listens for: it pages nobody, and a run that seems to pass does so partly because nothing looked at what it chose. Required fields and a close refusal make that record durable rather than an instruction that can be omitted. The obligation is keyed to a dial because how much of the operator's reading a run may claim is the operator's to set; it ships off, so a repository owes nothing until its owner asks. Every close owes the record, a partial close included, because every delegated run closes with one; a lane the machinery closed with no session present is refused too, and that refusal is a hold for a person to write the record rather than a strand. A malformed entry is reported rather than refused, because the record is there and readable and a refusal would hold finished work for a reporting defect. A decision is an already selected direction, so the operator either confirms it or overrules it; acceptance would falsely imply a new selection. A closed verdict makes … [1282 more chars — read the registry row]

## SR-227 — Where the retention dial is on, adjudication routes retain sessions and reset them only when safe

> **Requirement.** Where the declared adjudicator retention dial is above zero, the delivered adjudication content shall manage each retained-class adjudication consistently whether the coordinator or loop starts it: resume the route's active, unheld session in its model runner's resume form; mint and record a session id where the route has no active session; wait a bounded time where its active session is held, then run unretained and state why; record each call like any other; drain the session when its latest request's occupancy reaches the dial or the inputs it judges under change; retire it only when no work it has a stake in is pending, except that at the first keep after a blackout window it retires a session without a last use or last used before that window ended with reason blackout even when its chain is pending; retire it at once when a call fails except that an authentication failure records the failed call and leaves its session record unchanged; let no two calls use one retained session at once; write the retention state only whole, by one writer at a time; make any keep-warm call one bounded turn that never blocks the scheduler; compose both routes' retention request once; and, before retaining a family that uses a dedicated CLI home, read any long-lived credential at each launch from the location a declared environment setting names, keep every verbatim occurrence out of logs, records and commits, and refuse an absent or unreadable credential before launch, naming setup without… [336 more chars — read the registry row]

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that a configured agent implement toward the vision with no human curating what comes next; it does not name retaining a session, so this obligation arrives through three lenses rather than through the need's text. The performance lens listens for an operating-cost risk left unassessed: a run that spins a fresh adjudicator for every small work item reloads the spine on each call, and the usage record makes that cost visible, while resuming a session by id uses the provider's prompt cache. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a retained session that has filled, judges under changed rules, or is started in a home whose sign-in cannot be read degrades every later judgement with nothing paging anyone; a coordinator route that bypasses the retained operation repeats that cost and loses the state the loop preserves. One composition keeps both routes under the same drain and lease rules. The integrity lens listens for a durable artifact updated in place without an all-or-nothing guarantee and for a claim nothing can reclaim: two calls resuming one transcript at once corrupt it, so one call at a time holds a session, under a hold that expires when its holder is gone. Refusing an unreadable or absent sign-in before that hold prevents a failed retained launch from changing state or escaping into a person's default home. Retiring only when no work the session has a stake in is pending keeps a review, rework and … [503 more chars — read the registry row]


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

### LLR LLR-290
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: The codex adapter reads compacted entries and per-request input counts from the exact thread rollout under the launch CODEX_HOME, or codex's own default home when that is unset, and turn.completed running input totals from exec. Retained bookkeeping records compacted and compaction-source in the call row and compacted and compaction_source in the store: reported for a rollout entry, otherwise inferred from any decrease across consecutive new rollout requests, starting with the stored request_prompt baseline and the first new request. Only rollout per-request prompts drive inference; without a readable rollout there is no new inference, and exec turn sums are retained only as a running-total baseline. The rollout request cursor and latest prompt baseline live in the retained record, so old requests are not rechecked and a kit reset starts a new comparison; a resumed legacy record lacking a rollout cursor first learns the latest prompt and cursor before inferring. Reported evidence takes precedence over inferred evidence. Once recorded, compacted and its source hold for every later call of that retained session, though the call shows no new evidence; a reported entry replaces an inferred source, and an inferred drop never replaces a reported one.

Only rollout per-request prompts drive inference; without a readable rollout there is no new inference, and exec turn sums are retained only as a running-total baseline. The rollout request cursor and latest prompt baseline live in the retained record, so old requests are not rechecked and a kit reset starts a new comparison; a resumed legacy record lacking a rollout cursor first learns the latest prompt and cursor before inferring. Reported evidence takes precedence over inferred evidence. ### LLR LLR-305
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: REQUEST. AdjudicationRequest carries root, brief, the selected route row, work item, role, prompt-template overrides and deadline. Both callers compose that request once; adjudication_keep derives governing templates, then plans with the selected row. PREPARATION. prepare_launch reads no registry: from that row and the selected entry account it derives family, template, model, tier, route environment, account environment, account home and launch credential. A token account is signed-in exactly when the file its token_file_variable names is present, readable and nonempty; its probe runs no command. A login-account home resolves without being created. For a login account home, only documented command-run pairs count: Codex accepts exactly one Logged in using <method> line at exit 0 or exactly Not logged in at exit 1, apart from blank lines and its PATH-alias warning; Claude without a token accepts exactly one JSON object with boolean loggedIn true at exit 0 or false at exit 1; another answer, failure or timeout is unknown. An absent, unreadable or empty token file refuses before a lease, naming the declared variable and the setup step but never its path or value. refuse_competing checks every declared account: its route or account environment may not declare a credential source ranked above its account home; a token account also may not declare its token variable, and only a token account’s template may not carry --bare; names compare under platform case rules; settings-file credential sources are outside this boundary. compose_env builds the launch environment by removing names the launch sets and competing credential names, then applying route pairs, account pairs, home and token as the launch's one environment credential. Prepared carries the template's first token after normal substitution, resolved once to an absolute executable on that environment's PATH; failure refuses before a lease and names the runner and declared or ambient PATH. cli_version and the retained launch run that executable; _as_prepared makes act launch and account the selected row rather than caller copies. probe_env composes the prepared environment with the launch credential withheld. OUTPUT. act redacts each verbatim occurrence of the credential value from physical output lines and captured stream before result, usage, logs or records consume it; escaped, encoded and line-split representations are outside that bound. For a qualified OpenCode account, the environment starts from its declared platform and network-transport base plus kit-owned names, then route pairs, account pairs and home, never ambient environment; OPENCODE_AUTH_CONTENT is refused. No registry reread, fallback or automatic sign-in occurs. AdjudicationRequest also carries scope and account. governing_templates receives the request role: for REVIEW it returns the once-resolved reviewer template text used by the brief, and for ADJUDICATE it keeps the retained-class identity; adjudication_keep passes that role. The governing identity of a retained set holding amendment, first-acceptance, or combined also covers the act brief, by its wired override text when present and otherwise by its shipped text.
  - after: REQUEST. The adjudication request carries the root, the brief, the selected route row, the work item, the role, the prompt-template overrides, the deadline, the scope and the account. Both callers compose that request once; the shared keep step derives the governing templates, then plans with the selected row. PREPARATION. Launch preparation reads no registry: from that row and the selected entry's account it derives the family, template, model, tier, route environment, account environment, account home and launch credential. A token account is signed in exactly when the file its declared token-file variable names is present, readable and nonempty; its probe runs no command. A login account's home resolves without being created. For a login account's home, only documented command-and-exit pairs count: Codex accepts exactly one Logged in using <method> line at exit 0 or exactly Not logged in at exit 1, apart from blank lines and its path-alias warning; Claude without a token accepts exactly one JSON object whose logged-in flag is boolean true at exit 0 or false at exit 1; another answer, failure or timeout is unknown. An absent, unreadable or empty token file refuses before a lease, naming the declared variable and the setup step but never its path or value. The competing-credential check covers every declared account: its route or account environment may not declare a credential source its runner ranks above its account home; a token account also may not declare its token variable, and only a token account's template may not carry the runner's bare-mode flag; names compare under platform case rules; settings-file credential sources are outside this boundary. The launch environment is composed by removing the names the launch sets and the competing credential names, then applying the route pairs, the account pairs, the home and the token as the launch's one environment credential. The prepared launch carries the template's first token after normal substitution, resolved once to an absolute executable on that environment's search path; failure refuses before a lease and names the runner and the declared or ambient search path. The runner-version probe and the retained launch run that executable, and the launch step launches and accounts the selected row rather than the caller's copies. The probe environment is the prepared environment with the launch credential withheld. OUTPUT. The launch step redacts each verbatim occurrence of the credential value from physical output lines and the captured stream before the result, usage, logs or records consume it; escaped, encoded and line-split representations are outside that bound. For a qualified OpenCode account, the environment starts from its declared platform and network-transport base plus the kit-owned names, then the route pairs, the account pairs and the home, never the ambient environment; an OpenCode account declaring its auth-content variable is refused. No registry reread, fallback or automatic sign-in occurs. The governing-template step receives the request's role: for a review it returns the once-resolved reviewer template text the brief is built from, and for an adjudication it keeps the retained-class identity; the shared keep step passes that role. The governing identity of a retained set holding amendment, first-acceptance or combined also covers the act brief, by its wired override text when present and otherwise by its shipped text.

  - before: ENTRY. coordinator_adjudicate runs only from a claimed lane worktree and otherwise refuses before launch through IF-284. A non-zero exit, timeout or reported error result returns the failed-call outcome through IF-284 without reading the verdict; after a successful call, an empty reservation is a missing verdict recorded failed. Under the loop marker, each prompt it launches ends with loop provenance after its binding is read. The fresh verdict reservation, validation, and deadline conditions above apply to the judging turn; the act turn instead uses the later completion conditions stated here.

A launched call leaves its verdict file, empty or written, for inspection. The adjudicator act turn runs under the resolved entry’s declared account, the same account as its judging turn, so a retained act continues that session. The default route and every command outcome are declared by IF-283 and IF-284, and the command also exposes a sign-in reading. An act invocation from a claimed clean lane derives a complete later-turn brief from the verdict and binding committed at HEAD, plans retention by that verdict class, and launches and logs the adjudicator call without reserving a verdict or recording an outcome. It refuses before launch for a lane, route, uncommitted lane change, or act-derivation refusal, naming the relevant input; it succeeds only after a successful call leaves no authorized act owed and no changed verdict record, otherwise fails, and a sign-in refusal reports its distinct result. ## SR-229 — Coordinator claim admission respects a blackout pause

> **Requirement.** Where a blackout window is armed, the delivered work-item admission shall refuse a new claim inside that window on every route at any context-guard dial; outside a blackout window, where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, it admits a coordinator claim only from the current lease holder while that holder's context drain is not latched, the drain latching once the holder's context occupancy reaches the declared threshold and holding across compaction and resume until the operator records a clear or a release, the holder records a hand-back naming its handoff, or a relaunched successor takes the lease.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that a declared pause stop claiming and drain what is already in flight, and that recovery never double-assign work; it does not name the coordinating session's own context, so this obligation arrives through two lenses rather than through the need's text. The unattended-operations lens listens for a silent degrade: a coordinator whose context has crossed its declared limit keeps claiming after compaction has degraded the context it needs to close the work it already holds, and nothing pages anyone. The integrity lens listens for a recovery path written only for the unattended run when the same corruption is reachable attended, and for a claim nothing can reclaim once its holder is gone: a retained adjudicator session already drains on occupancy while the attended coordinator does not, and a second or missing holder leaves claim authority ambiguous, so one lease names the coordinator and only the operator's recorded release frees a lease whose holder has gone. A latched single-holder admission boundary was chosen over a one-time event observation because every claim must remain protected when an event is missed or the transcript later reports a lower reading. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name the coordinating session's own capacity as a declared reason to stop claiming and drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-300
- **Detail**
  - before: guard_config reads the declared coordinator threshold and window: a non-positive, out-of-range or malformed threshold is off and a malformed window reads as the default. read_occupancy reads the newest valid assistant usage after the live transcript branch's latest compaction boundary, never a sidechain record, counting input and cached input; a record whose usage is malformed, partial or zero is skipped, and no valid usage reads as unknown, never zero. A primary-checkout lease names one coordinator session and its transcript. take refuses another holder; release frees the lease and clear unlatches it, each requiring and recording the operator's reason; hand_back accepts only the holder session and a handoff carrying a session prompt, frees the lease, and records the handoff; it refuses another or unnamed caller, whatever handoff it names, while naming the operator's release; it refuses the holder's own pending relaunch request, while a previous holder's request does not block it and is left for session_end's foreign-request refusal; with the guard off it returns before reading or writing. Elapsed time never transfers ownership. on_session_start installs a session as holder, unlatched, only when it presents the successor token the holder's exit wrote into the lease, and restates a latched drain to the holder's own resumed start. Only the holder's own hook payload on the holder's transcript is measured, never a subagent's. latch_reading latches drain when the unrounded reading reaches the threshold and retains it through lower readings, compaction and resume; it ends only through the owner's clear, the operator's release, the holder's hand-back, or a successor take. window_refusal runs before the dial on every claim route, including the dispatcher, naming the UTC end; the dispatcher remains exempt only from the context guard. claim_refusal identifies the claimant by the session id the agent CLI exports to its tool processes (CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID), refuses with no lease held or a claimant that is not the holder, and reads and latches the holder's transcript itself, so a refusal never depends on a hook having run; a claim holding the dispatch lock never calls it. While a window is armed hooks act at every dial and deny the closed list of the main session's Agent call, SendMessage call, Bash/PowerShell model-CLI command word, and a Bash/PowerShell line unreadable only because of an unclosed single quote, unclosed double quote, unclosed POSIX dollar quote, unclosed POSIX backtick substitution, unclosed dollar-parenthesis substitution, or unclosed PowerShell here-string. One quote-aware shell reading per dialect reads quoted values as the shell does, and treats quoted separators, comments and here-document and here-string bodies as data. It finds the model-CLI command word after leading variable assignments, the timeout and env prefixes, the shell's reserved words, the chain operators and the call operator, and in substitutions written in the line or inside double quotes. It reads a PowerShell assignment to a variable as running its right-hand command, while a quoted or variable right-hand value stays data. A form outside this list gets no dedicated recognition: it may incidentally match this reading, and carries no guarantee either way. window_check exits nonzero inside an armed window. At every SessionStart inside a window, hooks inject the blackout close-down instruction; each main session's monitored events inject it at that session's first monitored event in the window and every twentieth thereafter, counted per session per window: bring each open lane to its pause point, using a wrap-up adjudication for an active claim where needed; write the handoff naming each lane and its next obligation; request no relaunch; and end the session. Hook responses inject the latch instruction once and bounded reminders thereafter; inside a blackout window the latch is still recorded, but the blackout close-down, which requests no relaunch, is injected in its place. PreCompact records trigger, occupancy and latch/request state; classify_compaction reports missed-threshold, manual and during-drain cases. Before recording a holder, the lease take refuses an inactive commit floor and records no holder. After identifying a session, the lease take first reads the guard hooks' state and records no holder unless it reads none or on, or a terminal consent installs them and the re-read reads on; without a terminal at absent, or at occupied, it refuses naming the consent step or the hooks to move.

Hook responses inject the latch instruction once and bounded reminders thereafter; inside a blackout window the latch is still recorded, but the blackout close-down, which requests no relaunch, is injected in its place. ## SR-230 — Coordinator successor at session exit outside a blackout pause

> **Requirement.** Where the declared coordinator context guard is enabled, when the coordinator lease holder that has requested a relaunch from its handoff ends its session outside an armed blackout window, the delivered coordination support shall start exactly one successor session from that handoff; inside the window it cancels the pending request and leave resumption to the operator.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 asks that claiming drain what is already in flight and that a crash at any lifecycle boundary recover without double-assignment; it does not name a coordinating session handing over to its successor, so this obligation arrives through the integrity lens rather than through the need's text. That lens asks what the next reader finds when a holder is gone: a close-out that loses its handoff strands the next coordinator, and one that can launch twice creates competing claim authority. An atomic, owned request consumed once was chosen over treating every session-end event as a relaunch because clear and resume do not end the coordinator and a failed launch must remain recoverable. Fed back to the need: SN-027's acceptance could name an orderly handover of the coordinating role as part of a drain; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-301
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: session_prompt extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. request_relaunch refuses inside a blackout window and otherwise accepts only the lease holder and an existing handoff, then writes a same-session request atomically. The hook's on_blackout SessionEnd dispatch calls blackout_session_end to cancel a pending request and record blackout inside a window; otherwise session_end acts only for a true exit by that holder in its declared repository, rejects a foreign request, and atomically renames one request to acquire it and holds the store lock from that acquisition until the launch is confirmed or the request restored, so restoration never waits on the lock. It writes a fresh successor token into the lease and the handoff prompt to a file, invokes the platform launcher detached in the declared root with that file and token, and on any failure before launch confirmation restores the request and removes the successor token and the prompt file. launch_command selects the Windows or POSIX launcher; launch_detached waits through its grace period and confirms only a still-running launcher or one that exits zero. Each launcher enters the declared root, resolves one interpreter by running its virtual-environment candidate followed by the candidates from its dev-setup's ordinary runtime search, and takes the first that reports Python 3.11 or later as its executable path; it exits nonzero with guidance when none resolves, runs window_check on that interpreter, exports the token as PT_COORDINATOR_TAKE for the successor's SessionStart take, and starts claude with the prompt as one argument: the POSIX one in a new terminal, the Windows one through exec_claude, which invokes claude with the extracted prompt. main dispatches the guard operations; reopening a session with the context latch still set needs the operator recorded clear.
  - after: The session-prompt reading extracts the one fenced session prompt from a handoff, preserving headings inside that fence as prompt text. ## SR-231 — Coordinator adjudication enters from a claimed lane and accepts only its current verdict

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordinator adjudication entry point shall, regardless of the declared adjudicator retention dial, run only from a claimed lane worktree and read success only from a verdict that its own call wrote within that call's deadline. From a claimed lane, it takes only an act an accepted current verdict authorizes. The verdict, reservation, and deadline obligations stated first apply to the judging turn; the act turn's success is the completion contract stated in the acceptance criteria.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks that a configured agent progress without a human curating what comes next; it does not state the entry boundary for an adjudication the coordinator starts, so this obligation arrives through two lenses. The unattended-operations lens listens for a run whose telemetry lands on trunk instead of the claimed work it judges, leaving a later reader unable to recover its work item context. The integrity lens listens for stale evidence being accepted as this call's success: a verdict left by an earlier or overdue call can make an unsuccessful current call look valid. A lane-local call that owns a fresh verdict reservation was chosen over trusting a caller-supplied path or a shared trunk record, because both leave the evidence's author and time ambiguous. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name attributable, current adjudication evidence as part of unattended progress; until it does, this row is derived and says so. An act is authorized only by an accepted, committed verdict, so it cannot substitute later work for the judgement that authorized it.

## SR-232 — Combined adjudication preserves each requested judgement

> **Requirement.** Where one lane checkpoint requests several declared adjudication kinds, the delivered adjudication content shall present and validate one sitting that preserves each kind's own scope, evidence and decision grammar.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-025 asks for unattended progress but does not require one sitting to carry several judgements. The unattended-operations lens catches a passing checkpoint where a requested kind was never judged; the integrity-recoverability lens catches a partial write where one kind acts while another section refuses.  A composed sitting was chosen so each judgement remains independently checkable while sharing its checkpoint. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name bounded, attributable checkpoint adjudication; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-310
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A combined class accepts only amendment:<id>, first-acceptance:<id> and done-when:<id> Adjudicates tokens; it composes each named class through compose with that section's verdict path in fixed amendment, first-acceptance, done-when order, and refuses whole if any section cannot compose. One parser reads machine lines one physical line at a time: every line whose first token is a machine keyword counts, complete or not, so a bare, split, bad-label or field-missing line invalidates. The same module's review_line is the one reader of a review VERDICT line: exactly one case-insensitive VERDICT keyword line is accepted only as `VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>`; the filing boundary refuses every other form and the merge reader treats it as no verdict. A combined sitting names exactly the kinds bound at reservation in <verdict>.requested, has exactly one kind-form lower-case section per bound kind and none other, and has across the whole file exactly one complete line per bound kind and one SITTING line, and no line for another kind; a single-kind verdict has no kind-form section and exactly one complete line for its kind. One binding records pending before either route calls: the coordinator entry point binds the request exclusively at reservation, and the loop binds its composed request at composition; write_binding is its one writer and record_outcome its one route recorder and commit path, returning its `(outcome, why)` decision and recording accepted only when call_succeeded has derived exit 0, no timeout and no reported error result, and validation passes. A dispute binding also records the findings file its composed brief names. It is written by the one writer in the same call as the requested ids, on either route. The outcome rewrite carries it unchanged, as it carries the identity cell. A brief that names none binds none, which every reader reads as absent. call_succeeded is derived once and shared by both routes; a failed call is recorded failed without its verdict being read, and the loop consumes that same why as its completion obligation. The coordinator validates its pending binding before recording its outcome, while the loop records against the kinds read back from its bound request; later consumers use the recorded-verdict reader, which returns only an accepted, bound, valid verdict and each part's row rulings. Every row an adjudication merge act flips needs an ACCEPT ruling in an accepted first-acceptance part, and every row it re-accepts needs a CLARITY ruling, or a MEANING ruling carrying the bless mark, in an accepted amendment part among the branch's own bindings, whether or not the act names a verdict; a recorded verdict over other rows authorizes nothing, and a named verdict must authorize each row its act re-accepts in the same way. Failed, pending, unbound or other-class verdicts cover nothing. Ordinary and mixed lanes consume the same claimed-row reader at the merge: raw scope parsing, the complete acted set including ledger-only acceptances, and the route’s normalized brief cannot give one consumer authority another does not see. Token and section parsing are shared by composition, validation, act scopes and merge-time minting; the class is routed and retainable through BRIEF_PROMPTS. An amendment verdict's `- [MEANING+BLESS]` row tag reads MEANING to every rulings reader, and a dedicated reader returns only rows so marked, treating a row without the mark or also tagged plain MEANING or CLARITY as not blessed. One reader of the rows a recorded verdict authorizes, a MEANING row only when so marked, serves both the merge and the act assembler.
  - after: A combined class accepts only amendment:<id>, first-acceptance:<id> and done-when:<id> Adjudicates tokens; it composes each named class through the shared brief composer with that section's verdict path in fixed amendment, first-acceptance, done-when order, and refuses whole if any section cannot compose. One parser reads machine lines one physical line at a time: every line whose first token is a machine keyword counts, complete or not, so a bare, split, bad-label or field-missing line invalidates. The same grammar's review-line reader is the one reader of a review VERDICT line: exactly one case-insensitive VERDICT keyword line is accepted only as `VERDICT: <APPROVE|CHANGES-REQUESTED> findings=<digits>`; the filing boundary refuses every other form and the merge reader treats it as no verdict. A combined sitting names exactly the kinds bound at reservation in the requested-kinds binding beside its verdict, has exactly one kind-form lower-case section per bound kind and none other, and has across the whole file exactly one complete line per bound kind and one SITTING line, and no line for another kind; a single-kind verdict has no kind-form section and exactly one complete line for its kind. One binding records pending before either route calls: the coordinator entry point binds the request exclusively at reservation, and the loop binds its composed request at composition; the binding writer is its one writer and the outcome recorder its one route recorder and commit path, returning its outcome with the reason for it and recording accepted only when the shared call-success derivation has found exit 0, no timeout and no reported error result, and validation passes. A dispute binding also records the findings file its composed brief names. It is written by the one writer in the same call as the requested ids, on either route. The outcome rewrite carries it unchanged, as it carries the identity cell. A brief that names none binds none, which every reader reads as absent. The call-success derivation is made once and shared by both routes; a failed call is recorded failed without its verdict being read, and the loop consumes that same reason as its completion obligation. The coordinator validates its pending binding before recording its outcome, while the loop records against the kinds read back from its bound request; later consumers use the recorded-verdict reader, which returns only an accepted, bound, valid verdict and each part's row rulings. Every row an adjudication merge act flips needs an ACCEPT ruling in an accepted first-acceptance part, and every row it re-accepts needs a CLARITY ruling, or a MEANING ruling carrying the bless mark, in an accepted amendment part among the branch's own bindings, whether or not the act names a verdict; a recorded verdict over other rows authorizes nothing, and a named verdict must authorize each row its act re-accepts in the same way. Failed, pending, unbound or other-class verdicts cover nothing. Ordinary and mixed lanes consume the same claimed-row reader at the merge: raw scope parsing, the complete acted set including ledger-only acceptances, and the route’s normalized brief cannot give one consumer authority another does not see. Token and section parsing are shared by composition, validation, act scopes and merge-time minting; the class is routed and retainable through the declared brief-prompt table. An amendment verdict's `- [MEANING+BLESS]` row tag reads MEANING to every rulings reader, and a dedicated reader returns only rows so marked, treating a row without the mark or also tagged plain MEANING or CLARITY as not blessed. One reader of the rows a recorded verdict authorizes, a MEANING row only when so marked, serves both the merge and the act assembler.

## SR-234 — Contested or repeated review findings receive a bounded adjudication

> **Requirement.** Where a review finding is contested by the builder or coordinator, or recurs for a third review round, the delivered adjudication content shall preserve each requested finding, the position held on it and its relevant change scope, accept only a verdict that rules every requested finding exactly once as a fix, a successor row, a reasoned dismissal in a declared class, or an escalation to the operator, and direct the dispute sitting to rule out a successor row for a fail-open, data-loss, or Done-when-gap finding.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-006 asks an unattended run to report failures clearly and surface to an operator when it cannot proceed, but it does not state who ends a contested or repeated review finding. The unattended-operations lens listens for an unbounded review loop in which the coordinator sends the same finding back to the builder without a bounded, attributable decision. A complete adjudication was chosen so an independent call can settle each requested finding without silently adding, dropping or defaulting one. Fed back to the need: SN-006's acceptance could state that contested review findings receive a bounded ruling; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

## SR-235 — Attended independent review verdicts are recorded or refused

> **Requirement.** When an attended launcher submits an independent review verdict for a work item's lane, the delivered review content shall record a verdict bound to the reviewed commit in the review-round record form the delivered verdict reporting reads, or refuse a malformed or unbound verdict without a record.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-024 requires an independent critical eye and assigns the attended reviewer to the operator, but does not require that verdict to survive as review history. The integrity-and-recoverability lens listens for a durable artifact updated without an all-or-nothing guarantee: accepting an unbound or malformed verdict can associate the wrong judgement with a lane, while dropping a recorded verdict leaves the next reader with no record of the independent review. A validated record-or-refusal boundary was chosen over treating submitted text as history because the latter cannot distinguish a verdict for the reviewed commit from unrelated text. Fed back to the need: SN-024's acceptance could name a durable attended-review record; until it does, this row is derived and says so.

## SR-237 — Blackout pauses new model work and resumes it after the window

> **Requirement.** When a declared blackout window is armed, the delivered coordination and unattended operation shall pause new model work until the window ends, preserving each claimed lane at its committed pause point and starting no model work inside the window except an adjudication needed to bring an active lane to that point, then resume eligible work after the window without retaining a session whose prior use predates that window's end.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-027 requires a declared pause to stop claiming and drain work already in flight without double assignment. The unattended-operations lens identifies a high-usage interval as a pause that must not discard lane state, while the integrity lens identifies a stale retained transcript as an unsafe substitute for a fresh resume. Keeping the active claim and committed evidence was chosen over a second held state because it preserves the recovery model.


### LLR LLR-300
- **Detail**
  - before: guard_config reads the declared coordinator threshold and window: a non-positive, out-of-range or malformed threshold is off and a malformed window reads as the default. read_occupancy reads the newest valid assistant usage after the live transcript branch's latest compaction boundary, never a sidechain record, counting input and cached input; a record whose usage is malformed, partial or zero is skipped, and no valid usage reads as unknown, never zero. A primary-checkout lease names one coordinator session and its transcript. take refuses another holder; release frees the lease and clear unlatches it, each requiring and recording the operator's reason; hand_back accepts only the holder session and a handoff carrying a session prompt, frees the lease, and records the handoff; it refuses another or unnamed caller, whatever handoff it names, while naming the operator's release; it refuses the holder's own pending relaunch request, while a previous holder's request does not block it and is left for session_end's foreign-request refusal; with the guard off it returns before reading or writing. Elapsed time never transfers ownership. on_session_start installs a session as holder, unlatched, only when it presents the successor token the holder's exit wrote into the lease, and restates a latched drain to the holder's own resumed start. Only the holder's own hook payload on the holder's transcript is measured, never a subagent's. latch_reading latches drain when the unrounded reading reaches the threshold and retains it through lower readings, compaction and resume; it ends only through the owner's clear, the operator's release, the holder's hand-back, or a successor take. window_refusal runs before the dial on every claim route, including the dispatcher, naming the UTC end; the dispatcher remains exempt only from the context guard. claim_refusal identifies the claimant by the session id the agent CLI exports to its tool processes (CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID), refuses with no lease held or a claimant that is not the holder, and reads and latches the holder's transcript itself, so a refusal never depends on a hook having run; a claim holding the dispatch lock never calls it. While a window is armed hooks act at every dial and deny the closed list of the main session's Agent call, SendMessage call, Bash/PowerShell model-CLI command word, and a Bash/PowerShell line unreadable only because of an unclosed single quote, unclosed double quote, unclosed POSIX dollar quote, unclosed POSIX backtick substitution, unclosed dollar-parenthesis substitution, or unclosed PowerShell here-string. One quote-aware shell reading per dialect reads quoted values as the shell does, and treats quoted separators, comments and here-document and here-string bodies as data. It finds the model-CLI command word after leading variable assignments, the timeout and env prefixes, the shell's reserved words, the chain operators and the call operator, and in substitutions written in the line or inside double quotes. It reads a PowerShell assignment to a variable as running its right-hand command, while a quoted or variable right-hand value stays data. A form outside this list gets no dedicated recognition: it may incidentally match this reading, and carries no guarantee either way. window_check exits nonzero inside an armed window. At every SessionStart inside a window, hooks inject the blackout close-down instruction; each main session's monitored events inject it at that session's first monitored event in the window and every twentieth thereafter, counted per session per window: bring each open lane to its pause point, using a wrap-up adjudication for an active claim where needed; write the handoff naming each lane and its next obligation; request no relaunch; and end the session. Hook responses inject the latch instruction once and bounded reminders thereafter; inside a blackout window the latch is still recorded, but the blackout close-down, which requests no relaunch, is injected in its place. PreCompact records trigger, occupancy and latch/request state; classify_compaction reports missed-threshold, manual and during-drain cases. Before recording a holder, the lease take refuses an inactive commit floor and records no holder. After identifying a session, the lease take first reads the guard hooks' state and records no holder unless it reads none or on, or a terminal consent installs them and the re-read reads on; without a terminal at absent, or at occupied, it refuses naming the consent step or the hooks to move.
  - after: The guard reads the declared coordinator threshold and window: a non-positive, out-of-range or malformed threshold is off and a malformed window reads as the default. The occupancy reading takes the newest valid assistant usage after the live transcript branch's latest compaction boundary, never a sidechain record, counting input and cached input; a record whose usage is malformed, partial or zero is skipped, and no valid usage reads as unknown, never zero. A primary-checkout lease names one coordinator session and its transcript. A take refuses while another session holds the lease; the operator's release frees the lease and the operator's clear unlatches it, each requiring and recording the operator's reason; the hand-back accepts only the holder session and a handoff carrying a session prompt, frees the lease, and records the handoff; it refuses another or unnamed caller, whatever handoff it names, while naming the operator's release; it refuses the holder's own pending relaunch request, while a previous holder's request does not block it and is left for the session end's refusal of a foreign request; with the guard off it returns before reading or writing. Elapsed time never transfers ownership. A session start installs a session as holder, unlatched, only when it presents the successor token the holder's exit wrote into the lease, and restates a latched drain to the holder's own resumed start. Only the holder's own hook payload on the holder's transcript is measured, never a subagent's. The drain latches when the unrounded reading reaches the threshold and is retained through lower readings, compaction and resume; it ends only through the owner's clear, the operator's release, the holder's hand-back, or a successor take. The blackout-window refusal runs before the dial on every claim route, including the dispatcher, naming the window's end in UTC; the dispatcher remains exempt only from the context guard. The claim refusal refuses with no lease held or a claimant that is not the holder, and reads and latches the holder's transcript itself, so a refusal never depends on a hook having run; a claim holding the dispatch lock never consults it. While a window is armed, hooks act at every dial and deny the closed list of the main session's subagent-launch call, subagent-message call, Bash or PowerShell model-CLI command word, and a Bash or PowerShell line unreadable only because of an unclosed single quote, unclosed double quote, unclosed POSIX dollar quote, unclosed POSIX backtick substitution, unclosed dollar-parenthesis substitution, or unclosed PowerShell here-string. One quote-aware shell reading per dialect reads quoted values as the shell does, and treats quoted separators, comments and here-document and here-string bodies as data. It finds the model-CLI command word after leading variable assignments, the timeout and env prefixes, the shell's reserved words, the chain operators and the call operator, and in substitutions written in the line or inside double quotes. It reads a PowerShell assignment to a variable as running its right-hand command, while a quoted or variable right-hand value stays data. A form outside this list gets no dedicated recognition: it may incidentally match this reading, and carries no guarantee either way. The guard's window check exits nonzero inside an armed window. At every session start inside a window, hooks inject the blackout close-down instruction; each main session's monitored events inject it at that session's first monitored event in the window and every twentieth thereafter, counted per session per window: bring each open lane to its pause point, using a wrap-up adjudication for an active claim where needed; write the handoff naming each lane and its next obligation; request no relaunch; and end the session. Hook responses inject the latch instruction once and bounded reminders thereafter; inside a blackout window the latch is still recorded, but the blackout close-down, which requests no relaunch, is injected in its place. Before a compaction the guard records its trigger, the occupancy and the latch and request state, and the compaction classification reports missed-threshold, manual and during-drain cases. Before recording a holder, the lease take refuses an inactive commit floor and records no holder. After identifying a session, the lease take first reads the guard hooks' state and records no holder unless it reads none or on, or a terminal consent installs them and the re-read reads on; without a terminal at absent, or at occupied, it refuses naming the consent step or the hooks to move.

### LLR LLR-316
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: blackout_at is the sole reader of the declared blackout value. At a supplied or current UTC instant it returns the declared value, whether the instant is inside an enabled interval, that interval's exclusive end, and the most recent interval end at or before the instant. It treats absent, malformed and equal endpoints as disabled, starts intervals only on weekdays, and assigns a midnight-wrapping interval to its start weekday.
  - after: The blackout reading is the sole reader of the declared blackout value. At a supplied or current UTC instant it returns the declared value, whether the instant is inside an enabled interval, that interval's exclusive end, and the most recent interval end at or before the instant. It treats absent, malformed and equal endpoints as disabled, starts intervals only on weekdays, and assigns a midnight-wrapping interval to its start weekday.

### TC TC-316
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Method**
  - before: Drive holder, other-session and subagent hook payloads and guarded claims with controlled transcript readings and lease state. Assert that a claim identifies its caller by the session variable the agent CLI exports, and refuses, naming its recovery action, with no lease held and from another or an unnamed session; that the holder below the threshold may claim; that a silent holder retains ownership; that the operator's release and clear each require and record their reason; that release and successor take transfer the lease; that the holder's hand-back frees the lease for a subsequent take, while another or unnamed caller, whatever handoff it names, the holder's promptless handoff, and the holder's own pending relaunch are refused, and a previous holder's leftover request after the operator's release does not block the current holder's hand-back; that the latch survives a lower reading measured by a hook or by a claim, compaction and resume, and ends only by the operator's clear or release, the holder's hand-back, or a successor take; that reminders are bounded; and that compaction telemetry records trigger, occupancy, latch and request state and is classified. Assert that a context latch tripped inside the blackout window, and the latched holder's resumed start inside it, each record the latch and are told only the blackout close-down, never a relaunch request, and that reopening the same session after the blackout with the context latch set still needs the operator's recorded clear.
  - after: Drive holder, other-session and subagent hook payloads and guarded claims with controlled transcript readings and lease state. Assert that a claim refuses, naming its recovery action, with no lease held and from another or an unnamed session; that the holder below the threshold may claim; that a silent holder retains ownership; that the operator's release and clear each require and record their reason; that release and successor take transfer the lease; that the holder's hand-back frees the lease for a subsequent take, while another or unnamed caller, whatever handoff it names, the holder's promptless handoff, and the holder's own pending relaunch are refused, and a previous holder's leftover request after the operator's release does not block the current holder's hand-back; that the latch survives a lower reading measured by a hook or by a claim, compaction and resume, and ends only by the operator's clear or release, the holder's hand-back, or a successor take; that reminders are bounded; and that compaction telemetry records trigger, occupancy, latch and request state and is classified. Assert that a context latch tripped inside the blackout window, and the latched holder's resumed start inside it, each record the latch and are told only the blackout close-down, never a relaunch request, and that reopening the same session after the blackout with the context latch set still needs the operator's recorded clear.
