## SR-238 — A deadline or interruption ends the session process tree

> **Requirement.** When a declared deadline or coordinator interruption ends a session, the delivered coordinator shall end every process in the session process group on POSIX or session job on Windows.

> **Rationale.** A descendant can outlive the direct session child and keep editing the worktree handed to the next session. Ending only the direct child was rejected because it leaves that concurrent writer outside the coordinator's supervision. The bounded guarantee excludes a descendant that leaves the session process group and a process another service starts outside the session job: neither is ended, and the kill does not report it. A POSIX zombie under a PID 1 that never reaps keeps the group nonempty, so the kill raises after the bound. A descendant that denies the kill access to wait on it also makes the kill raise, rather than letting the coordinator continue without knowing whether it ended. Stopping is the need's cannot-proceed case, not a supervision fault: continuing would hand the worktree to the next session beside a writer that may still be live, and nothing can proceed safely past that. Two windows lie outside the guarantee, each sub-second and on no supported workflow's ordinary path: an interruption while the session is still being launched, before the coordinator supervises it, which can leave a running child; and a process that joins the session job while the kill takes its member list, which is terminated with the job but can still be ending, for milliseconds, when the coordinator continues.


### LLR LLR-026
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: Runs the agent CLI headless: build_argv substitutes {model} into argv and, when a CmdTemplate carries no {prompt} placeholder (the default for both provider CLIs), routes the prompt to the child's STDIN (run_session writes it from a daemon thread then closes), while the no-wedge invariant holds — a fixed prompt plus EOF, never an interactive read. A codex session additionally captures that CLI's own --output-last-message file as the result (_codex_lastmsg_setup/_codex_lastmsg_read). stdin is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status.md-resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them. An interruption ends the tree only once the coordinator supervises the session; one that lands while the session is still being launched is outside the kill. A wall or idle deadline, or an interruption while the coordinator supervises the session, ends every process in the session process group on POSIX or session job on Windows: the direct child is reaped, then the group or job is polled until empty. On Windows the kill takes a handle on each member, then terminates the job, and returns only when the job count is zero and every held member is signalled; an unopenable live member raises after the terminate. Past 10 s the kill raises naming the survivor. A failure to end the tree, or to show that it ended, raises the named tree-end error; an ordinary launch error still returns the session error. A child that cannot be contained ends as a session error before it runs.
  - after: Runs the agent command-line tool headless: the launch substitutes the routed model into the command's tokens and, when the command template carries no prompt slot (the default for both provider tools), delivers the prompt on the child's standard input, written from a background thread and then closed, while the no-wedge invariant holds: a fixed prompt plus end-of-file, never an interactive read. A Codex session also captures that tool's own last-message file as the result. Standard input is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status-file resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them. An interruption ends the tree only once the coordinator supervises the session; one that lands while the session is still being launched is outside the kill. A wall or idle deadline, or an interruption while the coordinator supervises the session, ends every process in the session's process group on POSIX or its session job on Windows: the direct child is reaped, then the group or job is polled until empty. On Windows the kill takes a handle on each member, then terminates the job, and returns only when the job count is zero and every held member is signalled; an unopenable live member raises after the terminate. Past 10 s the kill raises naming the survivor. A failure to end the tree, or to show that it ended, raises the named tree-end error; an ordinary launch error still returns the session error. A child that cannot be contained ends as a session error before it runs.
- **Rationale**
  - before: A large prompt passed as argv dies at the OS command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows .CMD shim, 32767 via CreateProcess) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so stdin is the only delivery that survives — both limits verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that CLI echoes its banner and the whole prompt into stdout, so stdout is not the result. A surviving descendant can keep editing a worktree after its parent session ends; process-group and job containment make the tree an atomic session lifetime, while an uncontained Windows child is rejected before it can create that survivor. Holding each member before termination covers the measured interval where the job count reaches zero before a terminated process is signalled.
  - after: A large prompt passed on the command line dies at the operating system's command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows command shim, 32767 through direct process creation) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so standard input is the only delivery that survives; both limits were verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that tool echoes its banner and the whole prompt into standard output, so standard output is not the result. A surviving descendant can keep editing a worktree after its parent session ends; process-group and job containment make the tree an atomic session lifetime, while an uncontained Windows child is rejected before it can create that survivor. Holding each member before termination covers the measured interval where the job count reaches zero before a terminated process is signalled.

## SR-239 — Where the reviewer retention dial is on, loop review routes retain only their scoped reviewer sessions

> **Requirement.** The delivered loop review content shall, if the reviewer setting is malformed, or names a persisted final reviewer while retention is off, read it as retention off and a fresh final reviewer; otherwise, where the declared reviewer retention dial is above zero, resume a reviewer only across iteration rounds for one lane, claim and review phase, keyed by those values and the iteration stage; read an absent, unreadable, malformed, misfiled or differently keyed retained record as no session and start fresh; never keep a reviewer session warm; retire that iteration session when the lane's full-lane round is drawn; run that round fresh, or, where the declared final-reviewer setting selects persistence, on one retained final reviewer per lane and phase that never resumes an iteration session; treat a change to the template a retained reviewer's brief is built from as a change of its governing inputs; and record session-mode and keep-scope as audit-only columns.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement. The Performance, Unattended Operations and Integrity Recoverability lenses identify the operating cost, unattended judgement degradation, and shared-state corruption risk of repeatedly minting reviewer sessions. Repeated fresh reviewer rounds can exhaust the available plan, while scoped identity prevents one lane or review stage from inheriting another transcript. Coordinator-written review-scope and session-generation records, not reviewer-authored text, establish final-review provenance; session-mode and keep-scope remain audit-only. Fed back to the need: SN-025's acceptance could name bounded operating cost per reviewer judgement as part of what lets a run proceed with no human curating it; until it does, this row is derived and says so.


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

### LLR LLR-305
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: REQUEST. AdjudicationRequest carries root, brief, the selected route row, work item, role, prompt-template overrides and deadline. Both callers compose that request once; adjudication_keep derives governing templates, then plans with the selected row. PREPARATION. prepare_launch reads no registry: from that row and the selected entry account it derives family, template, model, tier, route environment, account environment, account home and launch credential. A token account is signed-in exactly when the file its token_file_variable names is present, readable and nonempty; its probe runs no command. A login-account home resolves without being created. For a login account home, only documented command-run pairs count: Codex accepts exactly one Logged in using <method> line at exit 0 or exactly Not logged in at exit 1, apart from blank lines and its PATH-alias warning; Claude without a token accepts exactly one JSON object with boolean loggedIn true at exit 0 or false at exit 1; another answer, failure or timeout is unknown. An absent, unreadable or empty token file refuses before a lease, naming the declared variable and the setup step but never its path or value. refuse_competing checks every declared account: its route or account environment may not declare a credential source ranked above its account home; a token account also may not declare its token variable, and only a token account’s template may not carry --bare; names compare under platform case rules; settings-file credential sources are outside this boundary. compose_env builds the launch environment by removing names the launch sets and competing credential names, then applying route pairs, account pairs, home and token as the launch's one environment credential. Prepared carries the template's first token after normal substitution, resolved once to an absolute executable on that environment's PATH; failure refuses before a lease and names the runner and declared or ambient PATH. cli_version and the retained launch run that executable; _as_prepared makes act launch and account the selected row rather than caller copies. probe_env composes the prepared environment with the launch credential withheld. OUTPUT. act redacts each verbatim occurrence of the credential value from physical output lines and captured stream before result, usage, logs or records consume it; escaped, encoded and line-split representations are outside that bound. For a qualified OpenCode account, the environment starts from its declared platform and network-transport base plus kit-owned names, then route pairs, account pairs and home, never ambient environment; OPENCODE_AUTH_CONTENT is refused. No registry reread, fallback or automatic sign-in occurs. AdjudicationRequest also carries scope and account. governing_templates receives the request role: for REVIEW it returns the once-resolved reviewer template text used by the brief, and for ADJUDICATE it keeps the retained-class identity; adjudication_keep passes that role. The governing identity of a retained set holding amendment, first-acceptance, or combined also covers the act brief, by its wired override text when present and otherwise by its shipped text.
  - after: REQUEST. The adjudication request carries the root, the brief, the selected route row, the work item, the role, the prompt-template overrides, the deadline, the scope and the account. Both callers compose that request once; the shared keep step derives the governing templates, then plans with the selected row. PREPARATION. Launch preparation reads no registry: from that row and the selected entry's account it derives the family, template, model, tier, route environment, account environment, account home and launch credential. A token account is signed in exactly when the file its declared token-file variable names is present, readable and nonempty; its probe runs no command. A login account's home resolves without being created. For a login account's home, only documented command-and-exit pairs count: Codex accepts exactly one Logged in using <method> line at exit 0 or exactly Not logged in at exit 1, apart from blank lines and its path-alias warning; Claude without a token accepts exactly one JSON object whose logged-in flag is boolean true at exit 0 or false at exit 1; another answer, failure or timeout is unknown. An absent, unreadable or empty token file refuses before a lease, naming the declared variable and the setup step but never its path or value. The competing-credential check covers every declared account: its route or account environment may not declare a credential source its runner ranks above its account home; a token account also may not declare its token variable, and only a token account's template may not carry the runner's bare-mode flag; names compare under platform case rules; settings-file credential sources are outside this boundary. The launch environment is composed by removing the names the launch sets and the competing credential names, then applying the route pairs, the account pairs, the home and the token as the launch's one environment credential. The prepared launch carries the template's first token after normal substitution, resolved once to an absolute executable on that environment's search path; failure refuses before a lease and names the runner and the declared or ambient search path. The runner-version probe and the retained launch run that executable, and the launch step launches and accounts the selected row rather than the caller's copies. The probe environment is the prepared environment with the launch credential withheld. OUTPUT. The launch step redacts each verbatim occurrence of the credential value from physical output lines and the captured stream before the result, usage, logs or records consume it; escaped, encoded and line-split representations are outside that bound. For a qualified OpenCode account, the environment starts from its declared platform and network-transport base plus the kit-owned names, then the route pairs, the account pairs and the home, never the ambient environment; an OpenCode account declaring its auth-content variable is refused. No registry reread, fallback or automatic sign-in occurs. The governing-template step receives the request's role: for a review it returns the once-resolved reviewer template text the brief is built from, and for an adjudication it keeps the retained-class identity; the shared keep step passes that role. The governing identity of a retained set holding amendment, first-acceptance or combined also covers the act brief, by its wired override text when present and otherwise by its shipped text.

## SR-240 — Commit-floor activation before agent routes

> **Requirement.** Where a checkout's commit floor is inactive, the delivered agent routes shall refuse to start and name the applicable repair that activates the floor.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement through the SECURITY lens: an agent route can create a governed commit, and an inactive commit floor leaves that action without the enforcement SN-005 expects. Start-time refusal was selected over a configurable bypass so the route cannot grant itself the authority the floor is meant to enforce.


### LLR LLR-340
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: For a checkout root, the commit-floor activation contract returns no refusal only when the resolved hooks directory is the checkout's or primary checkout's .githooks directory and both commit hooks can run; otherwise it returns a refusal naming the detected cause and its repair: activation for an unsuitable hooks path or unavailable Git answer, restoration for a missing hook, or executable-mode repair for an unrunnable hook. Each route applies that result before it creates route-specific work, and a session launch distinguishes this refusal from a blackout refusal.
  - after: For a checkout root, the commit-floor activation contract returns no refusal only when the resolved hooks directory is the hooks directory at the top of the checkout or of the primary checkout and both commit hooks can run; otherwise it returns a refusal naming the detected cause and its repair: activation for an unsuitable hooks path or unavailable Git answer, restoration for a missing hook, or executable-mode repair for an unrunnable hook. Each route applies that result before it creates route-specific work, and a session launch distinguishes this refusal from a blackout refusal.

## SR-241 — Review briefs render only over a matching verified passed-checks record

> **Requirement.** When an attended launcher requests a review brief for a lane commit, the delivered review content shall render it only when that commit, or a commit beneath it from which only record changes or no changes separate it, carries a verified record that the declared commit checks and the test set derived from the work item's requirements passed on its tree; otherwise it refuses without a brief and names the cause and the step that makes the record.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-004 and SN-008 require trustworthy gate outcomes, but do not say that a metered independent-review session must be withheld when its requested tree is already known not to meet its declared commit checks. The performance lens listens for operating cost left unassessed: a review over such a tree spends the scarce review session on a failure the process can name before dispatch, while a record-only change must not require the same checks again. Trusting the verified record was chosen over running the checks during rendering because rendering then stays a bounded reader of a prior result rather than a second executor with a potentially different result. The coordinator and unattended review routes decompose this shared boundary separately. Fed back to the needs: their acceptance could state that a review begins only from a verified commit-check result.

## SR-243 — Coordinator calls record sessions without committing outside claimed work

> **Requirement.** When the coordinator's tooling launches a model session, the delivered coordinator call capability shall record the session in the usage record, record its log without committing to the shared line of development when it starts outside claimed work, refuse an unsafe resumed authoring carrier before launch, judge the call's changes against its starting state, and provide the sole launch route for author, critic and REVIEW-A calls.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement, and labelled so. SN-026 requires attributable routing among model families, and SR-222 requires a usage record for sessions the delivered loop launches, but neither reaches a coordinator-started session or states where its record may be committed. The unattended-operations lens identifies a partial or missing call record that leaves unattended progress unrecoverable, while the integrity-and-recoverability lens identifies telemetry committed to unrelated shared work as evidence with ambiguous provenance. Recording every coordinator session through the same usage record while retaining an out-of-work log without a shared commit was chosen over leaving coordinator sessions unrecorded or treating every checkout as a commit authority. Fed back to the need: its acceptance could state that every coordinator-started model session has attributable usage and a recoverable record without changing unrelated shared work. SR-231's rationale supplies the lane-local provenance lens; its adjudication-only boundary remains unchanged.


### LLR LLR-269
_accepted — re-acceptance owed_
- **Detail**
  - before: A role hands a Call its data (root, role, the route's template, model and prompt, provider, tier, route id, source event, attempt id, further attribution, env, deadlines, console renderer, attached) and nothing else. act builds the argv (agent_session.build_argv), lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless through run_session or attached through run_attached, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with usage-source and usage-status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. record writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, the raw stream to raw_dir/raw_name when given, and its own telemetry commit. call is act then record with the standard call_<invocation-id> row, recording an interrupted or failed launch too. The worker loop (launch_session, then record with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats (_dp_session) all launch through it; nothing else calls run_session, run_attached or the session-log writer. The service identity row accounts every call with session-mode, resumed only when its keep resumes a session, and keep-scope, the keep scope or empty; write_session_log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record operation commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.
  - after: A role hands the service a call carrying only the data that call differs by, including the root, the role, the route's template, model and prompt, the provider, tier and route id, the source event, attempt id and further attribution, the environment, the deadlines, the console renderer and whether it is attached; the service owns every launch and record step. The launch step builds the command line from the template, model and prompt, lets the launched runner's adapter prepare it, launches it headless or, for an attached call, attached to the terminal, reads the final text through the adapter, and fills the accounting: the invocation id and timestamps, wall seconds, exit code and timeout kind, the adapter's usage record with its usage source and usage status (known when input and output are both reported, partial when anything is, else unavailable), and the latest request's session id and context occupancy; a launch that raises is accounted with unavailable usage and re-raised. The record step writes one session log from the caller's row overlaid by that accounting, with the whole captured stream as its transcript, writes the raw stream to the file the caller names when it names one, and makes its own telemetry commit. The call step is the launch step then the record step with the standard call row, named for the call's invocation id, recording an interrupted or failed launch too; a step its caller hands it runs with the launch's outcome, or with none when the launch raised, after the launch and before the log is written. The worker loop (its launch, then the record step with the row it composed), the hands-on sitting, the route probe and the dual-plan hats all launch through it; no other module calls the headless or attached session launch or the session-log writer. The call's identity accounting records its session mode, fresh unless its retained session resumes one or it resumes an unretained thread, and its keep scope, the retained scope or empty; the session log records these as audit columns that no gate or scheduler reads. The record step commits a log only from a claimed linked worktree, including a worktree whose branch claim has closed while the shared tree still carries its active work; from the shared checkout, a detached checkout, or an unclaimed branch, it writes the log to the primary checkout's spool and commits nothing. An unreadable claim read adds no claim; it spools only when neither readable tree establishes the linked worktree's claim. A spool write failure raises without a commit; a refused telemetry commit is reported to the caller, the record step handing a caller that asks the log's path, whether it was spooled or committed, and the commit's refusal; and a non-empty resumed thread identifier is refused with retained-session input before accounting. A non-empty unretained resume identifier records the resumed session mode without retaining that identifier. Standard call-log names remain distinct from gate-log names.

## SR-244 — Transcript-derived usage records include coordinated nested agents

> **Requirement.** The delivered coordination capability shall record once each transcript-bearing model session that coordination completion ingests for coordinated work, including every session an agent spawns at any nesting depth, with its identity and transcript-derived usage; record no already-recorded message, duplicate no service record, grant its record no gate authority, include no transcript text, refuse an unknown source shape, and direct completion to ingest the current session and a named prior-session tail.

> **Rationale.** A DERIVED requirement through the PERFORMANCE lens: SN-026 makes model-family routing a configurable operating choice, but does not require the cost of coordinated sessions outside the launcher result to be visible. The lens identifies an incomplete cost reading when nested work is omitted or a repeated or resumed read inflates it. Recorded probes establish that a resume appends to its existing transcript, that a Claude result excludes sub-agent usage, and that arbitrary nesting occurs; a sub-agent's final hand-back often has no stop record and is complete once the session's transcripts show the agent finished. The direct-child fan-out re-stamp is intentional: the independently observable reader, identity, contribution, recovery, duplication, shape, source-selection, record, CLI, and completion decisions must not be merged. Fed back to the need: its acceptance could require the operator to see complete, non-duplicated usage for coordinated work across the configured families.
